In the name of God, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful

Praise be to God, Lord of all the worlds.

<!-- page 2 -->
Part One (A)

From the compendium known as

*Iṣāf al-Ṭālib fī Jamīʿ al-Maṭālib*
(The Student's Aid in All Demands)

Composed by our master and Mawlānā Shams al-Kayān, the proof of his age,
Fayḍ ʿAlī ibn Sayyidinā Sulaymān —
may God sanctify their souls.

This is the sixth composition of its author, in which he gathered religious rulings and arranged them according to the pillars of Islam.

<!-- page 3 -->

<!-- page 4 -->
In the name of God, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful

Part One (A)

*Iṣāf al-Ṭālib fī Jamīʿ al-Maṭālib*
(The Student's Aid in All Demands)

By our master and Mawlānā Shams al-Kayān, the proof of his age,
Fayḍ ʿAlī ibn Sayyidinā Sulaymān —
may God sanctify their souls.

The sixth composition of its author, in which he gathered religious rulings and arranged them in the order of the pillars of Islam.

<!-- page 5 -->
By Him I seek help, upon Him I rely, and He is sufficient for me. My success lies only with God; in Him I place my trust, to Him I turn in repentance, and to Him I commit my affairs. God is ever with His servants, and there is no power except through God, the Most High, the Most Mighty.

Praise be to God — the fulfillment of every hope for the people of truth, guidance, and certainty; the one who makes the features of religion clear to those who have embraced it, and who made His saints ladders of salvation for those who aspire to rise to the heights of true life. I praise Him and bear witness that there is no god but He, the one before whose greatness all creation is humbled. I bear witness that Muḥammad is His servant and messenger — sent with the choicest of laws, whose noble character contained the arts of removing innovations — and I send blessings upon his brother, guardian, and the Commander of the Faithful among those who descend from the sacrifice, Ismāʿīl ibn Ibrāhīm al-Khalīl, who in his interpretation dispels the suspicions that have gathered around the apparent revelation. And upon the niche of his light and the vessel of his inner and outer truth — Fāṭima al-Batūl, daughter of the Prophet Muḥammad — and upon her eldest son, Abū Muḥammad al-Ḥasan al-Muṭahhar, and upon her second son, the master of the knowledge of the seven oft-repeated verses of the great Quran, Abū ʿAbdullāh al-Ḥusayn, who excels in the knowledge of the two paths, and upon the pure Imams from his descendants, the righteous guides of his lineage — the Princes of the Faithful, the family of Ṭāhā — the good and honest trustees of God Almighty, custodians of His revelation,

<!-- page 6 -->
the suns of souls and the masters of great knowledge, those who stand by their command and make plain their secret before the Imams, the revealers of strength — the Commanders of the Faithful, the firm rope of God, inheritors of their fathers, inspirers of His saints, noble preachers in every age, and faithful keepers of the sciences that would otherwise collapse without support. Among them, and belonging to that age, the one who in his time depicted the images of the people of faith: Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān ibn al-Ḥasan ibn Yūsuf ibn Sulaymān, guardian of his fathers and, before them, one of the foremost preachers — along with his noble friend Mawlānā the Imam al-Ṭayyib and his pure sons, the station of Isḥāq and his children and Ismāʿīl and his descendants, upon all of whom be the prayers of the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. And upon his successor — first as deputy, then as the beloved and glorious preacher Muḥammad ibn al-Faḥd — and peace upon them all until the Day of Judgment.

After praising God Almighty and glorifying Him, and sending prayers upon His messenger, his family, and those who call to them: a great pillar of the boundaries of religion and a firm support for the believers, steadfast in loyalty against blame and deception, continues — through the passing of days — to speak of the people of the neighborhood of our master, our guide, our father, and our refuge, the unrivaled and time-honored caller, the radiant and glorious Sheikh, the dear and holy figure who has no equal in his age. He strove tirelessly

<!-- page 7 -->
in reviving the religion of God Almighty — the firstborn of authentic honor, of both world and faith, the caliph of his trustworthy father, the proof of Mawlānā the Commander of the Faithful, and his overseer among the people of the daʿwa. All of us turn to Jaʿfar ibn Sulaymān — may God sanctify him in the highest stations — who showed us mercy, intercession, and approval, and bestowed upon us his grace and blessing. And we turn again to the faithful successor, the one who is noble in his father's daʿwa, whose sword of daʿwa strikes at the very crown of the head — he is a living presence among us: our master, our Mawlānā, our father, our refuge, the great preacher and noble Sheikh Muḥyī al-Dīn, destroyer of all enemies, the radiant Sheikh and mighty temple, our master the preacher Muḥammad ibn al-Faḥd — may God sanctify him in the glorious station and grant us his mercy, approval, and intercession with his guardian and protector.

I gathered, for him and for those from among the faithful who seek religion and wish to gain understanding in the doctrine of the Ahl al-Bayt of the Prophet, Lord of all the worlds, what had been scattered across the books of God's saints — peace be upon them — and their learned scholarly preachers: paired books and brief abstract works dealing with matters of Sharīʿa and legal opinion, composed in accessible language along the paths of jurisprudence and legal opinion, so as to bring the reader closer and make the traversal of these paths easier. I therefore called upon God Almighty

<!-- page 8 -->
and upon His guardian — upon him be the finest prayers and peace — and upon the preacher and the guardian of my grace, the most exalted of God in the stations of light. The guardian of my grace was a storehouse and sponsor — may God sanctify him in the great station — and I responded to his request and gathered what he sought. I began this work to the full extent of my effort, energy, diligence, and ability, and in light of what I owe those who have shown me grace and favor; no task of this kind was beyond reach.

Indeed, it comes from the material of my victorious grace, which closes no door to me, from the storehouse of my blessing — and the one who holds claim over me deserves nothing less than this. I set to work, carrying through both night and day whatever errors and mistakes arose; these are the failings of one who falls short, and I delegate my affairs to them and rely upon them in everything, for they are my sufficiency. Their spiritual presence brings healing, and the beginning of this composition fell on the seventh day of the month of Rajab al-Aṣabb — itself the seventh month of the year of awakening from heedlessness — in the fifty-first year after the great thousandth milestone of the pure migration of the Prophet, upon whom be the finest prayers and the purest greeting. I named this book in keeping with what was said above:

*Iṣāf al-Ṭālib fī Jamīʿ al-Maṭālib* — The Student's Aid in All Demands — for that is its definition and its design, as it is its name. I pray to the guardian of my aforementioned grace and to his storehouse that they will guide all my affairs well, in this and in every other path, that they will accept my work, inform me through their generosity of good hope, seal my life with their approval, and make my final abode with them, through their virtue. Both of them are the enemies of whoever opposes our master Muḥammad and his pure family, upon whom be the prayers of God. I worship Him alone and seek help from Him, for He is the Powerful, the Mighty, the Compeller. There is no power except in God, the Most High, the Most Great — and God guides whoever He wills to a straight path.

<!-- page 9 -->
**The Chapter of Faith**

**The Book of Guardianship**

It is stated in some of the noble books: "Know, my brother, that faith has eight levels."

The first of these is charity toward parents, relatives, orphans, and the needy — the neighbor who is a relative, the neighbor who is nearby, the companion at one's side, the traveler, and those whom your faith obligates you to care for. This is established in the Book of God, the Exalted.

He then said: Whoever imitates the worship of God and the call of the Ahl al-Bayt — peace be upon them — without knowledge and without action, has no right to claim the name of faith, and has no covenant or honor before God or before His guardian, peace be upon him, for he has departed from it.

A mention of the distinction between faith and Islam, and what falls under each, and a mention of otherwise.

Our master and Mawlānā stated — the trust of the Imam is the banner of Islam; may God sanctify him and grant us his intercession and his blessing in his noble councils, which are designated the Councils of Exposition and the Councils of Wisdom, known as the Mustaṇṣiriyya Councils; may God's peace be upon their author — in the fourth council of the Quran, where he said: It has been said that the Imams of religion — peace be upon them — are seven, recurring in this world as Fridays recur until the Resurrection, while the twelve proofs appear in the world. To know all the limits of this — meaning the limits of the testimony of God, most holy — is to perform its rights, which consist of seven duties for religion and twelve obligations for the pious.

<!-- page 10 -->
The first seven are: (1) Guardianship, (2) Purity, (3) Prayer, (4) Zakāt, (5) Fasting, (6) Ḥajj, (7) Jihad.

The twelve are: (1) Honoring parents, (2) Maintaining ties of kinship, (3) Protecting the neighbor, (4) Enjoining good, (5) Forbidding evil, (6) Truthfulness in one's home, (7) Good conduct with one's spouse, (8) Kindness to those in one's care, (9) Spreading peace, (10) Feeding others, (11) Building brotherhood, (12) Visiting the sick. These are not obligatory for women.

Mawlānā the Commander of the Faithful ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib — peace and blessings of God be upon him — said in his four hundred precepts: The Children of Israel divided into seventy-two sects, and this nation will divide into seventy-three — one of them in Paradise and the rest in the Fire.

**A Mention of the Guardianship of the Commander of the Faithful, ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib — peace be upon him — his love, and his station**

Our master and Mawlānā, the long-serving preacher Jaʿfar ibn Manṣūr al-Yaman — may God exalt him and sanctify him and grant us his intercession and his blessing — said in the Book of the Foster-Suckling:

"Muḥammad is his prophet — God is his master, Jibrīl and the righteous of the believers" — meaning the Imam of the believers, who is ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, the Imam of every believer. He said: "Their father was righteous" — meaning an Imam. And he said: "The earth is inherited by My righteous servants" — meaning the Imams from among His servants. And Mawlānā the Imam al-Muʿizz li-Dīn Allāh — peace be upon him —

<!-- page 11 -->
said in the Friday sermon, likening himself in the physical world to a pen in the spiritual world, and set four ranks beneath it so that before each one a spiritual rank guides it toward the one beyond. He thus established the foundation facing the tablet, engraving in it what he derived from the spiritual world, just as a pen engraves in a tablet.

**A mention of the answers given by the Commander of the Faithful, ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib — peace be upon him — to questions he was asked, and what followed from them.**

It is stated in the book *Uns al-Afidah al-Muniba*, in the account of one of the cases of the Commander of the Faithful ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib — peace be upon him — and his remarkable rulings:

The king of Rūm sent a messenger to Medina with a sum of money and instructed him: "Go to Muḥammad. If he does not agree with you, then ask him about his successor, and put to him proof." He was to ask three questions, and if answered, he was to hand over the money.

The man arrived in Medina and was brought before the Messenger of God — may God's peace and blessings be upon him — and asked about his successor. The Prophet indicated Abū Bakr. They brought him forward and the man asked him: "Tell me what is not God, what is not with God, and what God does not know." Abū Bakr grew angry and said to him: "Woe to you — by saying this you have added to your disbelief." The man left his presence. He was then directed

<!-- page 12 -->
to ʿUmar, who responded in much the same way. Then ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said: "What kind of men are these — they could not answer his questions, and yet they did not simply say, 'We do not know'? Instead they accused him of knowing falsehood. I know who knows the answer." He was asked, "Who is he?" He said, "ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib." They said, "Take us to him," and they all set out together with the man and knocked on the door.

The Commander of the Faithful — peace be upon him — came out to them with a pen behind his ear and his finger stained with ink. ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās told him the story, and he — peace be upon him — said to the man: "Ask what you wish." The man said: "Tell me what is not God's." He said: "God has no partner." He said: "Tell me what is not with God." He said: "Injustice." He said: "Tell me what God does not know." He said: "That the Messiah is the son of God — for God Almighty, glorified and exalted be His name, does not know it," meaning it does not exist and God has no knowledge of what does not exist.

The man said: "I bear witness that there is no god but God, that Muḥammad is the Messenger of God, and that you are his guardian." He then handed over the money, and ʿAlī — peace be upon him — gave it to al-Ḥasan and al-Ḥusayn — peace be upon them — and told them: "Go and distribute it among the Muslims," and they did as he commanded.

It is also narrated from Abū al-Jārūd, from al-Ḥārith al-Awar, that while the Commander of the Faithful — peace be upon him — was in al-Raḥba with the people around him, a man stood and addressed him: "Peace be upon you, O Commander of the Faithful, and the mercy and blessings of God." The Commander of the Faithful looked at him — peace be upon him — and said: "And upon you peace, and the mercy and blessings of God. Who are you?" He said:


<!-- page 13 -->
A man from your flock and the people of your country. He said to him, "Are you from my flock or from the people of my country? If you greet me for a single day, you would have no fear of me." Then he said to those around him: "Do you know this man?" No one knew him. So he said to the man, "What do you know about yourself, that even if I had seen you before, you would not have feared me?" The man said, "Safety, O Commander of the Faithful." He said, "Have you committed any transgression since you entered this world?" He said, "No." He said, "Perhaps during war?" He said, "Yes." He said, "Then if war is the occasion — the man said: Mu'awiyah sent me to you and commanded me to come to you. I am a messenger, sent to ask you about things that Ibn al-Asfar sent to him to inquire about. He says to you: if you are the rightful guardian of this matter and the true claimant of the caliphate after Muhammad, then answer me these questions — for if you do, I will follow you and send you the jizya. But when the Messenger found no answer to them, he sent me to you, hoping you would know." He said, "And what are they?" He said, "How great is the distance between truth and falsehood? How great is the distance between heaven and earth? How great is the distance between east and west? And what is this galaxy? And what is the rainbow? What is the darkening of the moon? And what is the first thing that appeared on the face of the earth? And what is the first thing that moved upon it? And what is the spring to which the souls of the polytheists flee? And what is the hermaphrodite? And what are the ten things, each harder than the last?" The Commander of the Faithful said to him a prayer of God.

He said: "God has destroyed the son of the liver-eater — he has gone astray, and those with him have gone astray, and God has

<!-- page 14 -->
freed his slave girl. How excellent is God's decree, to have set between me and this nation those who severed my ties of kinship, wasted my days, denied me my right, diminished my station, and conspired against me in my dispute." Then Ali said, "Al-Hasan, Al-Husayn, and Muhammad" — they came to him. He said to the man, "O brother of the people of Damascus, these are the sons of the Messenger of Allah, God bless him and his family" — and he gestured toward al-Hasan and al-Husayn, peace be upon them — "and this is my son" — and he gestured toward Muhammad, may God be pleased with him — "so ask whichever of them you wish." The Syrian said he wished to ask al-Hasan, peace be upon him. The Commander of the Faithful, God's prayers be upon him, instructed him, and al-Hasan was asked about all these questions.

Al-Hasan, peace be upon him, said: "O brother of the people of Damascus, the distance between truth and falsehood is four fingers — what your eyes see is truth, and what your ears may hear is often falsehood." The Syrian said, "You are right, may God set you aright." He continued: "And the distance between heaven and earth is the prayer of the oppressed, and the reach of sight. Whoever says otherwise has lied." He said, "You are right." He said, "And between east and west is the arc of the sun in a single day — observe it as it rises, and observe it as it sets. Whoever says otherwise has lied." He said, "You are right." He said, "As for this galaxy, it is the lights of the sky, from which rain descends. And the rainbow is God's bow, and a sign of safety from drowning. As for the darkening of the moon — the light of the moon was once like the light of the sun,

<!-- page 15 -->
but God blotted it out. And God Almighty said regarding that: ⟪ar:وَجَعَلْنَا اللَّيْلَ وَالنَّهَارَ آيَتَيْنِ فَمَحَوْنَا آيَةَ اللَّيْلِ وَجَعَلْنَا آيَةَ النَّهَارِ مُبْصِرَةً⟫ — 'And We made the night and the day two signs, and We erased the sign of the night and made the sign of the day visible.' As for the first thing that appeared on the face of the earth, it is the valley of Dab. And the first thing that moved upon the face of the earth is the palm tree. And the spring to which the souls of the polytheists flee is the spring known as Salma. As for the hermaphrodite, it is a human being in whom it is unknown whether they are male or female — one examines them: if they have had a wet dream as a man does, or if they menstruate as a woman does, or if their breasts develop. Or it is said: if urine strikes a wall, then they are male; if it recedes as a camel's urine recedes, then they are female. As for the ten things, each harder than the last — the hardest thing God created is stone; harder than stone is iron, which cuts through it; stronger than iron is fire; stronger than fire is water; stronger than water is cloud; stronger than cloud is wind; stronger than wind is the angel; stronger than the angel is the Angel of Death; and more severe than the Angel of Death is death itself — and more severe than death is the command of God, Lord of all worlds."

Then the Syrian said, "I bear witness that you are the son of the Messenger of God, peace be upon him and his family, the guardian of Muhammad, peace be upon him, and that you are of greater standing than Mu'awiyah." He wrote these things down and departed. Mu'awiyah sent it to Ibn al-Asfar, and Ibn al-Asfar said: "I bear witness that this knowledge does not come from you — it is from the very substance of prophecy and the seat of the message. Were you to ask me for a single dirham, I would not give it to you."

It was also narrated from him that he said: 'Umar ibn al-Khattab came to the Commander of the Faithful, peace be upon him, and said to him, "O Abu al-Hasan, there are matters I neglected to ask the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, about —

<!-- page 16 -->
do you have knowledge of them?" He said, "And what are they?" He said: "A man lies down and sees something in his dream — if he pays attention, it is as vivid as if he held it in his hand; yet he may see something else that is nothing at all. A man meets another man and loves him without knowing why. A man meets another man and hates him without knowing why. And a man who sees or hears something and remembers it for a long time, then forgets it when he most needs it, then recalls it at another moment of need."

The Commander of the Faithful, peace be upon him, said to him: "As for what you say about a man seeing something in his dream — God, Blessed and Exalted, said in His noble Book: ⟪ar:اللَّهُ يَتَوَفَّى الْأَنفُسَ حِينَ مَوْتِهَا وَالَّتِي لَمْ تَمُتْ فِي مَنَامِهَا فَيُمْسِكُ الَّتِي قَضَىٰ عَلَيْهَا الْمَوْتَ وَيُرْسِلُ الْأُخْرَىٰ إِلَىٰ أَجَلٍ مُّسَمًّى⟫ — 'God takes souls at the time of their death, and those that have not died, during their sleep; He keeps those for whom He has decreed death, and releases the others until an appointed term.' There is no servant who lies down to sleep except that a likeness of death comes over him. Whatever he sees while his soul is separated from his body and journeying through the realm — that is truth. Whatever he sees upon the soul's return is false and is the delusion of Shaytan. As for your saying that a man sees another and loves him without knowing why, or hates him without knowing why — it is because God created souls before bodies by two thousand years. They dwelt in the air and used to meet and mingle like horses. What they came to know of one another then, they will know today and love; and what they came to dislike of one another then,

<!-- page 17 -->
they will dislike today and hate. As for your saying that a man sees or hears something, then forgets it, then remembers it, then forgets it again — there is no heart that does not carry a scar like the scar of the moon." When the heart is covered over, the servant forgets what he has seen and heard.

So 'Umar said, "You are right, O Abu al-Hasan. May God not spare me a land in which you are not present and in which your name is not mentioned."

A mention of the guardianship of the Imams — the family of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him. A mention of the obligation of praying for Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon them. And what pertains to prayer, the good proclaimers, the righteous borders, their stations, and the command to return peace and to reply to the greeting of the book.

He said in the twenty-third council of the Mustansiriya councils, may God's peace be upon the author of the book: he has finished mentioning the Sunnah that we stipulated discussing — namely, the revelation of peace. It is divided above into seven sections: what came from God and from His angels. Below it, it is divided into twelve sections: what came from human beings. The highest, the first, the most particular, the most exalted, and the most sincere is the word of God Almighty: ⟪ar:سَلَامٌ عَلَيْكُمْ⟫ — "Peace be upon you." The second, which carries some figurative quality, is His saying: ⟪ar:يَا نُوحُ اهْبِطْ بِسَلَامٍ مِّنَّا⟫ — "O Noah, descend in peace from Us." The third, which is more suggestive, is His saying about their greeting on the day they greet Him with peace. The fourth is His saying about them conveying greetings and peace. The fifth expresses that it is from the angels in particular, when He says: ⟪ar:وَالْمَلَائِكَةُ يَدْخُلُونَ عَلَيْهِم مِّن كُلِّ بَابٍ سَلَامٌ عَلَيْكُم⟫ — "And the angels enter upon them from every gate: peace be upon you." The sixth is that Allah and His angels pray for the Prophet, peace be upon him: ⟪ar:يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا صَلُّوا عَلَيْهِ وَسَلِّمُوا تَسْلِيمًا⟫ — "O you who believe, pray for him and submit to him in full submission." The seventh is the word of the Almighty: ⟪ar:سَلَامٌ عَلَىٰ آلِ يَاسِينَ⟫ — "Peace be upon the family of Yasin." And Yasin is our Prophet Muhammad, peace be upon him and his family, and the family of Yasin is the family of Muhammad, may God's prayers be upon them.

He said in the twenty-fourth assembly of it:

<!-- page 18 -->
Know that the revelation of peace is an obligatory Sunnah of Islam, one that speaks of affection and submission — and Salam is one of the names of the One possessed of majesty and honor. You have heard what we have read to you from what it contains regarding the seven sections, and this is followed by the twelve divisions, which concern the greeting exchanged between human beings.

The first of these is the greeting of the people of the right hand to the faithful prophet, peace be upon him and his noble family. He says — and he is the most truthful of speakers: ⟪ar:سَلَامٌ عَلَيْكُم بِمَا صَبَرْتُمْ⟫ — "Peace be upon you for your patience" — for if they are among the people of the right hand, then peace be upon them from the people of the right hand. The second is the greeting upon entering the mosque, with peace, because the Almighty says: ⟪ar:فَإِذَا دَخَلْتُم بُيُوتًا فَسَلِّمُوا عَلَىٰ أَنفُسِكُمْ تَحِيَّةً مِّنْ عِندِ اللَّهِ مُبَارَكَةً طَيِّبَةً⟫ — "When you enter houses, greet one another with a greeting from God, blessed and good." The third is the peace of one who concludes the prayer. The fourth is the peace of the believer in the face of the ignorant — He said: ⟪ar:وَإِذَا خَاطَبَهُمُ الْجَاهِلُونَ قَالُوا سَلَامًا⟫ — "And when the ignorant address them, they say: peace." The fifth is what the Sunnah specifies: that the rider on a camel initiates the greeting to the rider on a horse. The sixth is that the rider on a horse initiates the greeting to the rider on a donkey. The seventh is that the rider on a donkey initiates the greeting to one walking. The eighth is that the one walking initiates the greeting to one standing. The ninth is that the one standing initiates the greeting and then returns it — and the return of the greeting is divided into three kinds: the virtuous reply, the complete reply, and the just reply. As for the virtuous reply, it is to greet whoever initiates the greeting with something better than what they offered. The complete reply is to respond in kind, as God Almighty said: ⟪ar:وَإِذَا حُيِّيتُم بِتَحِيَّةٍ فَحَيُّوا بِأَحْسَنَ مِنْهَا أَوْ رُدُّوهَا⟫ — "And when you are greeted with a greeting, return it with something better, or return it as it is." And the just reply is in responding to the dhimmis, for they do not initiate the greeting — and if they do initiate it,

<!-- page 19 -->
one responds: "And upon you" — because it was reported that some of them used to deceive Muslims in their greetings, saying ⟪ar:السَّامُ عَلَيْكُم⟫ — "Death be upon you" — instead of ⟪ar:السَّلَامُ عَلَيْكُم⟫. So the Sunnah came to say in response: "And upon you" — a just reply, for whoever said something good received its like, and whoever said something bad received its like.

Our master Amir al-Mu'minin, the front of the religion of God, peace be upon him, said in his prayer on Sunday: after praying for the higher borders, "O God, reach the lower borders — the five: the spokesman, the foundation, the culmination, the successor, and the wing." And he said, peace be upon him, in his prayer on Saturday: "O God, upon the seven days and the twelve months, send the purest, most honorable, and most noble of prayers." Some of them said — the saints of the religion, may God sanctify their souls — in praying for the borders: in his prayer on Sunday: "O God, reach the proclaimers and their gates, the imams of their stations, their proofs, and their preachers." And he said in his supplication on Monday: "O Allah, pray for the seven proclaimers, the seven and eighteen, the seven caliphs, and the twelve successors — the purest prayer, the most growing, the most honorable, the highest, and the most significant. And admit us into all the good into which You have admitted them, and spare us from every evil from which You have spared them." And he said in his supplication on Tuesday: "Reach Your manifest borders and Your righteous guardians, the spokesmen who call to Your light, the proven foundations of the truth of Your religion, the culmination, the successor, and the wing — through whom the gates of Your mercy were opened." And he said in his prayer one day —

<!-- page 20 -->
Wednesday: "O God, upon them and upon their gates, their proofs, and their prayers — send the best of Your holy prayers." And he said in his prayer on Thursday: "O God, bless the days and months, the hours of the day and the hours of the night, both the manifest and the hidden." And he said in his prayer on the Sabbath: "Dedicate to Muhammad, O God, and to the Imams of his station and their proofs — their remembrance, many times over what You have prayed for them all."

And our master Amir al-Mu'minin, Allah's peace be upon him, said in his Four Hundred Counsels: "When someone greets you, return the greeting with something better than it, or return it in kind. Shake hands with your enemy if he dislikes it — for God has commanded His servants to do so, as the Almighty says: ⟪ar:ادْفَعْ بِالَّتِي هِيَ أَحْسَنُ فَإِذَا الَّذِي بَيْنَكَ وَبَيْنَهُ عَدَاوَةٌ كَأَنَّهُ وَلِيٌّ حَمِيمٌ⟫ — 'Repel with that which is better, and then the one between whom and you there was enmity will become as though he were a close friend.' And he said therein: 'If your brother says, "May God greet you with peace," then say: You are the peace of God, and the finest of abodes and stations.'"

He said in the book of the question — which is from the late Sheikh Shamoun ibn Muhammad Ghori al-Hindi, may God be pleased with him — and the answer, which is from our master Badr al-Din, our master Hasan ibn our master Idris ibn Sayyidna al-Hasan ibn Sayyidna Abdullah al-Alaf al-Aqsa, may God sanctify him and grant us his intercession — and the question, to summarize: "What do you say, may our souls be ransomed for you, about what has been revealed to us — the Shaytan's whispering regarding submission to sheikhs, professors, and those close to them? Most of the people of India do not proclaim peace with their hands, but instead bow or lower their heads intending to submit to them. Some say this resembles the practice of unbelievers. O our master, please provide an answer in this matter" —

<!-- page 21 -->


<!-- page 21 -->
The answer to those who ask whether there is a ruling on this matter is: offering peace is better. The question was posed: What do you say, O you who have illuminated the jewels of our souls with the light of knowledge, regarding the returning of a greeting — how much of it does a Muslim hear, and is it obligatory? Is it obligatory to respond when one hears a Muslim's greeting? In the case of sneezing, is it obligatory to say the blessing upon hearing the sneezer say "Praise be to God," or immediately upon hearing the sneeze itself? And must the blessing be said in the same measure as what the sneezer said, or not? The answer is: none of this is required by duty. One may do as one wishes, and there is no fault in leaving any of it — except for replying to a greeting, whether aloud or silently.

The statement concerns what has been transmitted from the Imams of the family of Muhammad, peace be upon him. Our master Ja'far bin Mansour al-Yemen said — may God sanctify him — in the book of the Esoteric Breastfeeding, commenting on the verse "the earth is inherited by My righteous servants," that this refers to the Imams among His servants. He also said, regarding the verse "and We wished to be gracious to those who were oppressed in the land and to make them Imams and make them the heirs": by "those who were oppressed" he means the descendants of Ali ibn Abi Talib — they were made weak in the land, meaning in the very person of Ali ibn Abi Talib himself — and he is the proof of Muhammad, and the earth is as he is. And our master al-Mu'izz li Din Allah, peace be upon him, said in his supplication on Sunday, in which he erected without the foundation the completion of the prayer of Israfil.

<!-- page 22 -->
Mentioning the houses of the Imams — may God's blessings be upon them — their conditions, their innocence from any alteration of their station, and their expiation from whatever falls short of their rank, peace be upon them.

Maulana Imam al-Mu'izz li Din Allah, peace be upon him, said in his supplication on Sunday, in which he erected without the foundation what was completed in the face of Israfil. And Maulana Ali ibn Abi Talib, may God's peace be upon him, said in his supplication known as the supplication of victory and reverence — which contains the six aphorisms and the seven that are observed — in it: "And by the nine verses, and by the ten." In the passage where he said "And by the twenty-one," he said: "And by the seven Imams, and the seven caliphs, and the seven witnesses, and the seven alternates." And he said, God's peace be upon him, in his four hundred manners: "Whoever holds fast to us is right, and whoever holds fast to other than our way is right."

Mentioning the houses of the Imams' proofs, preachers, boundaries, and emissaries.

Maulana Imam al-Mu'izz li Din Allah, peace be upon him, said in his prayer on Sunday, in which he erected without the suffix in the face of Michael, and erected without the suffix of the wing in the face of Gabriel. Then he said: "These three were in their performance the respondents of the Speaker and the foundation in the physical world, just as the three were in the performance of the two origins — which are the Pen and the Tablet — in the spiritual world." By this he means — may God's peace be upon him — the grandfather, the conqueror, and the imaginer, who are Israfil, Michael, and Gabriel. And he said, peace be upon him, in his supplication on Friday and in the hudud:

<!-- page 23 -->
the Speaker, the Foundation, the Terminus, the After, the Wing, the Authorized, and the Respondent. And the twelve proofs in the hudud are the twelve, as God Almighty said: an oasis for human beings, with nineteen over it. And Maulana Amir al-Mu'minin said, may God's peace be upon him, in the prayer of victory and reverence: "And by the eight barefoot at Your Throne, O Lord of the worlds." He said: "By the eleven who prostrate, and by the twelve who are the counted kneeling ones." Our master Muhammad bin Tahir — may God sanctify him and grant us his intercession and forgiveness — said in a great prayer of his: "I beseech You, O God, by their doors, their proofs, their preachers, their authorized ones, the respondents of their roles, those who incline toward them, those who take refuge in their refuge, those who come to them, and all their followers." And Maulana the Commander of the Believers, Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him, said in his four hundred manners: "Whoever wishes to know what his standing is with God Almighty, let him see how God stands in his own sight." Among the sayings recorded in it: "The believer wearies himself while the people find rest through him." And he said: "Let the people of Paradise look upon the houses of our Shi'a as one of you looks upon a planet in the heavens."

Mentioning the prohibition against exaggeration, extremism, and so on.

Maulana Amir al-Mu'minin Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him, said in his four hundred manners regarding exaggeration concerning us: "Say that we are elevated servants, and say of our bounty what

<!-- page 24 -->
you wish." He said: "If the truth becomes clear to you, do not be hasty in your defense, for we accompany the one who draws near and follow the one who falls short."

Mentioning the commandments of the Imams — may God's prayers be upon them — to their guardians, and the commandments of their preachers to their Shi'a.

Maulana Amir al-Mu'minin Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him, said in his four hundred manners: "Whoever loves us, let him do our work and seek the help of the pious, for that is the best provision in this world and the next. Do not sit in shame on our behalf, and do not praise us openly before our enemy so that our love becomes apparent. Conceal your hatred of them and humble yourselves before their authority. Hold fast to the truth, for it is salvation. Desire what is with God, seek His pleasure, obey Him, and be patient in that. How ugly it is for a believer to enter Paradise while he is burdened — burdened by us having to plead and intercede for him on the Day of Resurrection because of what he put forward. Do not expose yourselves when your enemy is present on the Day of Resurrection. Do not allow them to reduce your standing with God through the pettiness of this world. Hold fast to what God has commanded you to pursue, so that between one of you and happiness — and the sight of what he loves — nothing stands except witnessing the Messenger of God, peace be upon him and his family. What is good with God is the best and most lasting, and you shall come to it with glad tidings; God will close his eye in peace until the meeting with God." He said: "Beware of disputation, for it breeds doubt." He said: "Measure your words against your deeds, and let your words be no more than good." He said: "Rush to do good before you are distracted

<!-- page 25 -->
by other things. Let most of your words be the remembrance of God Almighty. Beware of sins, for a servant commits a sin and thereby has his sustenance withheld from him." He said: "The best deed of the believer is waiting for relief." He said: "Happy is the one who takes admonition from another — so take admonition."

Mentioning the affection of the Imams, peace be upon them, and the longing of their allegiance and loyalty toward their preachers.

He said in the nineteenth council of the Mustansiriya councils, may God's peace be upon its author: "Listen now to what we have to say about what God has commanded regarding kinship ties, within which there are seven and twelve. The seven are those singled out by God's command in relation to the kinship of the Messenger of God, may God's peace and blessings be upon him, for he said: 'Say, I do not ask you for a reward for it except affection in kinship' — and they are the Imams of the religion, may God's prayers be upon them. And I have been promised seven things that recur like Fridays."

Mentioning the affection of the believers for one another and related matters.

Maulana Amir al-Mu'minin Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him, said in his four hundred manners: "Do not despise the weakest of your brothers, for whoever despises a believer is despised and hated by God. They will not be brought together on the Day of Resurrection except in repentance. Let none of you be burdened on behalf of his brother — ask him if he knows what he needs, visit him, show compassion, and be humble. Do not be like a hypocrite who describes what he does not do." He said: "A Muslim is the mirror of his Muslim brother. If you see a fault in your brother, do not treat him as a fool — guide him,

<!-- page 26 -->
advise him, be gentle with him, make the guidance pleasant, and show him favor, kindness, and mercy." He said: "O you who engage in backbiting — a Muslim does not violate the honor of his brother. God has forbidden that, and He said: 'Would any of you like to eat his brother's flesh dead?' Think of that." He said: "When you meet your believing brothers, greet them with a handshake and show them joy. When people part from one another, let no burden of grievance remain between you."

And it is stated in the book of questions — which comes from the late Sheikh Shimon bin Muhammad al-Ghuri al-Hindi, may God be pleased with him — and the answer from our master Badr al-Din, Sayyidina Hassan bin Sayyidina Idris bin Sayyidina al-Hassan bin Sayyidina Abdullah al-A'la, may God sanctify him and grant us his intercession and forgiveness. The question: What do you say — may God illuminate our concerns with the light of guidance — regarding the practice common among the people of India, whereby some of them ask others for water, salt, food, and the like, considering it permissible without payment and without the means to obtain it elsewhere, instead asking those who have it: is this permissible for them or not?

The answer: This is fine, for the sake of affection and brotherhood.

Mentioning the righteousness owed to spiritual and physical parents, and related matters.

He said in the seventeenth council of the Mustansiriya councils, may God's peace be upon its author: We have already explained what we mentioned of the seven obligatory rights.

<!-- page 27 -->
These seven correspond to the seven chapters in the testimony — a number that matches its chapters without dispute — and the remaining twelve correspond to its approved years, which in turn correspond to the number of its letters, as we have explained in our definition of it. The first of these is righteousness toward parents. He said: "God Almighty says, 'And We have enjoined upon man goodness toward his parents — his mother carried him with hardship and gave birth to him with hardship'" — thus recommending goodness toward both parents, while singling out the mother by her actions and not the father. He said in another place: "And We have enjoined upon man goodness toward his parents — his mother carried him and weaned him," and again the mother is singled out by her actions in both verses. This is God's wisdom in distinguishing her with honor and righteousness. He said in the eighteenth council: We had finished mentioning the righteousness of parents in terms of what God has singled out for the mother — an additional rank in righteousness and dignity. So listen now to what you will read: there are seven obligatory sections and twelve literary duties in righteousness toward parents. The believer must honor his father and mother, who are the cause of the appearance of his physical form. They labored in feeding him with the finest and most nourishing of foods until he reached a point of hope and flourishing. And the righteousness of his parents in religion — who are the cause of the appearance of his spiritual form — they are the one who precedes time, peace be upon him, and his proof. And the righteousness of the father of this community, who convey divine sciences — that is, the Prophet and the guardian, may God bless them, and the Imams from their descendants. It is narrated from the Messenger of God, may God's peace and blessings be upon him, that he said: "And you, O Ali, are the father of this community." This righteousness concludes with one showing the community its path,

<!-- page 28 -->
achieving it, exalting it, drawing it close, cautioning it, attaching it to the right, and saving it from what would keep it back — and saving it from what would harm it. As for the twelve literary duties in the righteousness of parents, they are: honoring their resting place, standing when one sees them, answering their call, lowering one's voice in their presence, magnifying them, not reproaching them, lowering for them the wing of humility from mercy, not preceding them in light or in darkness, not displaying to them anything of one's affairs, not saying to either of them an unkind word, wishing them long life as they wished him long life when he was young, and doing enough for each of them in need as they once did for him when he needed them. He said in the twenty-fourth council among them: There are three rights that are obligatory — 1. the right of God, 2. the right of parents, and 3. the right of the child. God has commanded that these be fulfilled and will not accept them unless they are complete. God's right is to refrain from what He has forbidden and to enter into what He has commanded. The right of parents is to give thanks for what has passed and to obey them in what remains. The child's right is to be raised with compassion, nourished with food, and taught the Book and wisdom. Maulana Amir al-Mu'minin Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him, said in his four hundred manners: he disobeyed his parents.

Mentioning the affection owed to religious and worldly relatives and related matters.

He said in the nineteenth council of the Mustansiriya councils, may God's peace be upon its author: as for a person's relatives, they are divided into twelve categories, the first of which is:

<!-- page 29 -->
1 — Children, male and female. 2 — Those born of the same father and mother, who are full siblings and are called brothers. 3 — Those born of the same father but different mothers, known as paternal half-siblings. 4 — Those born of the same mother but different fathers, known as maternal half-siblings and called uterine brothers. 5–6 — The paternal grandfathers and grandmothers. 7–8 — The maternal grandfathers and grandmothers. 9 — Paternal uncles. 10 — Paternal aunts. 11 — Maternal uncles. 12 — Maternal aunts.

Whoever is connected to one of these categories takes that category's ruling. The one who is commanded to maintain ties with them is obligated to be among those who are good to them and to uphold kinship bonds, to give to them and not deprive them, to draw close to them and not drive them away — and if they abandon him, Maulana the Commander of the Believers Ali ibn Abi Talib, may God's peace be upon him, said in his four hundred manners: "Do not swear an oath in the severance of the womb."

Mentioning the affection owed to religious and worldly neighbors, what is prohibited, and related matters.

He said in the twentieth council of the Mustansiriya councils, may God's peace be upon its author: We have mentioned the righteousness of parents and of kinship ties in what we have cited from the Sunnahs of the religion. Among these is the protection of the neighbor. God says: "And the neighbor who is near, and the neighbor who is distant, and the companion at your side." This is divided into seven sections and twelve subsections. The first is: 1 — Keeping faith with the believing neighbor who is close to you — he has three rights: the right of neighbor, the right of kinship, and the right of faith. 2 — The second is one between whom and you there is only the bond of neighborliness and lineage, with no bond of faith — he has two rights:


<!-- page 30 -->
the right of neighborliness and the right of kinship. 3- The third is the neighbor who is a believer but has no kinship with you — he holds the right of neighborliness and the right of faith. 4- The fourth is the companion at one's side: the neighbor who violates religion and doctrine — he holds the right of neighborliness alone. 5- The fifth is the companion on a journey: whoever has traveled at your side for a period of time holds the right of a neighbor. 6- The sixth is your companion in your caravan. 7- The seventh is your companion in gatherings where wisdom is heard — these are the most binding and obligatory with respect to the dhimmi. As for the twelve points of neighborhood etiquette: 1- A neighbor does not peer into his neighbor's house. 2- He does not eavesdrop on his neighbor's speech inside his home. 3- He does not disclose to others what he learns of his neighbor's affairs. 4- He does not let his neighbor hear what that neighbor would hate to hear. 5- He does not raise before his neighbor what that neighbor would hate to see. 6- He does not withhold help when his neighbor asks for it. 7- He does not act arrogantly toward his neighbor if he is of higher station. 8- He does not envy his neighbor. 9- He assists his neighbor when needed. 10- He conceals his neighbor's 'awrah if it becomes apparent. 11- He does not harm his neighbor, even when provoked. 12- He bears his neighbor's harm with patience.

These are the deeds of a people whose hearts Allah has tested with piety.

He mentioned the desires, the pursuit of knowledge, the Qur'an, and related matters.

Mawlana Amir al-Mu'minin Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him, said in his four hundred maxims of conduct: Do not let the night pass in silence except in the remembrance of Allah, and do not speak after the Hijrah. He said: A sane person should not recite the Qur'an without being in a state of ablution until he has purified himself.

26

<!-- page 31 -->
Mention of what must be learned, what one desires to know, what one refuses to articulate, and related matters.

Mawlana Amir al-Mu'minin Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him, said in his four hundred maxims of conduct: Do not let the procrastinator gain the upper hand over you in his view. He also said, in the book of questions attributed to the late Sheikh Shamoun ibn Muhammad Ghori al-Hindi — may Allah be pleased with him — and the answers from our master Badr al-Din, our master Hasan ibn our master Idris ibn Sayyidina al-Hasan ibn Sayyidina Abdullah al-Anf, may his intercession benefit us: The question was posed — What do you say, enlightened one, regarding the truths owed to the deserving? In that context, our master Hamid al-Din, may Allah sanctify his soul, mentioned in the book al-Hadi guidance through conclusive proofs, as did our master al-Nu'man — may Allah illuminate his tomb — in the book of supports. The preacher said: the finest expression is the complete liberation set forth by Sayyidina Idris ibn al-Hasan, may Allah bless him, in the book Zahr al-Ma'ani. He also stated, with the sincere prayers of Allah upon him, that al-Shafi'i was released from the silence of our master — did his condition change as did the conditions of al-Mughirah and Abu al-Khattab and their like, or is this a slander invented by the ignorant in their claim that the Imamate belonged to Muhammad ibn al-Hanafiyya and Abdullah ibn Muawiyah? Those two were never called to this for themselves — they responded with an answer: Is a sufficient summary not enough for those who have insight into religion? The answer was: Yes, it was released to him with approval, by the grace of the Ahl al-Bayt, peace be upon them, and nothing else. He also said in a book

27

<!-- page 32 -->
of the aforementioned questions: Question — What do you say, O school of insights and authoritative pronouncements, regarding the saying of the Prophet, peace be upon him and his family, that whoever looks into his brother's book without permission is as though he looks into fire? Our master, when the people of a school gather together and look into this book and that book without seeking permission — what is the meaning of this saying? Answer: This applies to the Book of Truth, which a person may look into only with the permission of the one who gave it to him. In other cases it is a matter of etiquette, not obligation.

He also said in it — Question: What do you say, O apple of our eyes, about a man who asks another man to lend him the Qur'an or other books, and the bare cover is visible — is it permissible for him and for those who see it to repair and restore it without the permission of the owner, or not? Answer: There is nothing wrong with repairing it, and it is not obligatory for him.

The question was asked: What do you say, O door of our hoped-for goals, which none may reach except those who stand before it — regarding a man who died and left behind da'wah books and other belongings, and who owed debts to people that his estate cannot cover — should the books be kept in storage, or should his debts be settled with them? Answer: If the books are among the books of the da'wah, he has no right to use them for that purpose, because they belong to the call and are not inherited.

He also said in the questions addressed to some of the sheikhs of India, and in letters to some of our loyalists — the preachers of Yemen, may Allah sanctify them and may their intercession benefit us and earn us forgiveness: Question — May the exaggerated recite the beneficial obligations concerning mahrams and others in a single gathering? Answer: If there is

28

<!-- page 33 -->
a screen between the man and the woman through which she can hear what he is saying without seeing him, and there is no occasion for seclusion — that is, he is not alone with a woman without either another man accompanying him or another woman with her — it is permissible. He said in the question:

As for what you asked about the obligatory knowledge that cannot be neglected — it is what Allah Almighty has pledged to His servants: knowledge of His unity, declaring Him free from likeness, from postponement, and from deficiency; acknowledgment of the sending of the first and last prophets, and the testimony that conveys their message; acknowledgment of Muhammad, the Seal of the Prophets and Master of the Messengers, and belief in what he has conveyed; acknowledgment of the Imams from his descendants whom Allah Almighty has commanded us to follow and to answer in obedience, just as He commanded obedience to His Messenger — for He said: Obey Allah and obey the Messenger and those in authority among you. This includes knowing the Imam of the age, believing in his Imamate and his guardianship, submitting to his command, and taking knowledge from him. Allah Almighty has not left the earth without a proof for Him against His servants and an Imam who calls them to His obedience, for He said: He has no partner on the day when We shall call every people by their Imam. And to Muhammad, peace be upon him and his family: You are only a warner, and to every people there is a guide. Whoever knows this, believes it, affirms it and its truthfulness — it is upon such a person to follow the evidence, pursue the traces of the Prophet, and seek knowledge from them and take from them. Allah said: I am the most truthful of those who speak, so ask the people of remembrance if you do not know. As for the believer, once he has acknowledged and affirmed, his rank rises and his station is elevated. He said: Allah will raise those of you who believe and those who have been given knowledge by

degrees. And Ali, the Commander of the Believers, peace be upon him, said: The worth of every person

29

<!-- page 34 -->
is what he excels in. And the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, said: Seeking knowledge is obligatory upon every Muslim man and woman.

Mention of the prohibition of analogy in religion

Mawlana Amir al-Mu'minin Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him, said in his four hundred maxims of conduct: Do not measure the religion by analogy, for it cannot be measured. There will come people who measure the religion by analogy — they are the enemies of the religion, and the first to be tormented is Satan. He also said in it: If you hear from our discourse something you do not recognize, return it to us, stand by it, and receive it with trust.

Mention of the obligation to refer back in all that causes pain to Allah, to the Messenger, to his guardian, and to the Imams — prayers of Allah be upon them — and to their callers, may Allah's peace be upon them.

Our master Qadi al-Nu'man ibn Muhammad said in his book known as the Keys of Grace, and the truthful one — may Allah's prayers be upon him — said: I do not compel you to take some of our sciences and then return it to us. Do not reply to us — meaning, do not use what we have conveyed to you to argue against us before we have established its proof for you and taught you its interpretation. Do not reply to us by fabricating lies against us and turning away from us.

Mention of the command to conceal knowledge, secrets, religion, and piety.

The Commander of the Believers, Ali ibn Abi Talib, said in his four hundred maxims of conduct: Whoever broadcasts our secret — Allah will give him a taste of the iron.

30

<!-- page 35 -->
The Book of Purity

Mention of the circumstances that necessitate ablution

He said in the holy book known as the Book of Question and Answer, arranged according to the pillars of Islam and their doors, and the root of the issues, addressed to some of the scholars of the Indian island and in letters to the preachers of the Yemeni island — he mentioned it in order, collected it from its sources, and arranged it accordingly. He was also among the sheikhs of India who lived in the recent age. Allah Almighty has made it obligatory upon all those in authority who lead others toward obedience to the Imam and the sanctification of souls — the preachers who serve their Imams — and His prayers upon the Imams, who are the wellsprings of knowledge and wisdom. Those who follow His Book and knock at its doors are the family of the Prophet and the sons of the virgin, the children of the guardian and the descendants of al-Husayn — may Allah's grace encompass them and His peace and blessings include us in their intercession.

Question: What do you say, O finest of those who have brought down upon us the light of His knowledge — regarding something that has troubled our minds: it is mentioned in the book of purity that it is forbidden for a man to urinate beneath fruit trees. O our master, what is meant by these trees — trees that actually bear fruit, or any tree that has the characteristic of bearing fruit? Answer: Trees in which fruit is present, or those possessing the characteristic of bearing it.

Question: What

31

<!-- page 36 -->
do you say — and I have longed to meet him since I came of age, and it has been a long time — regarding what is mentioned in the books of the da'wah: that it is forbidden to address someone who is in a state of impurity, and to speak in such a condition? O our master, if the narrator of the da'wah is standing, is it permissible for him to speak in that place with a visitor or anyone else, and may greetings be conveyed to him or not? Answer: If he is standing, it is permissible for him to give salaam, and the other party must return the greeting and may speak.

Question: What our master says — may Allah bless him — regarding a stone used as a diuretic aid, with which a man cleans himself after urinating, and then it dries and he uses it again — is that permissible or not? Answer: There is nothing wrong with it, provided no trace of impurity or moisture remains on it.

He also said in the book Mukhtasar al-Musannaf: what nullifies ablution is sleep; the exit of anything from the passage whether wind or otherwise; enemas; fainting; madness; and the disappearance of the glans into a living or dead body.

He said in the book of the commentary on the pillars: it is also stated that if a person performing ablution is interrupted, he may continue from where he left off, unless the water has dried on the limbs that have already been washed.

As for the Mukhtasar al-Musannaf — regarding one whose hand has been amputated at or above the elbow, and nothing remains below it — the obligation of washing the hands is waived for him. Our master said:

And Mawlana, the trusted one of the Imam, the scholar of Islam — may Allah be pleased with him — in his annotated gatherings, the gatherings of exposition and wisdom known as the Councils of al-Mustansiriyya — may Allah bless its owner — said in the fifth council of it: You have heard what was recited to you; we have not singled out for you therein the glad tidings alone — we have given you from it the substance of what Allah

32

<!-- page 37 -->
Almighty said: among them are glad tidings in this life and in the hereafter, and among us is glorification — nineteen in conjunction with the chapters of the testimony, its seven and its twelve letters. Islam rests upon the seven obligations that follow upon twelve years. This is not hidden from one who knows and discerns — so listen now to what is recited to you.

For each of the aforementioned obligations there are seven and twelve Sunnahs, including the guardianship, which is the first and finest of them. It may be said that every reference to these numbers points to the order of the guardians of Allah — that is the sum and substance of what is meant and intended.

The guardianship is purity, and it has seven obligations and twelve Sunnahs. The seven obligations of ablution — which is the lesser purity — are: 1- Pure water, 2- Intention, 3- Washing the face, 4- Washing the hands, 5- Wiping the head, 6- Wiping the feet, 7- Order: beginning with what Allah has begun by mentioning.

The twelve Sunnahs are: 1- Invocation, 2- Washing the hands before inserting them into the vessel, 3- Pronouncing the name of Allah when sitting down for ablution, 4- Rinsing the mouth, 5- Inhaling water into the nose, 6- Moving the ring so that water reaches beneath it, 7- Combing through the beard, 8- Drawing water from the eyes, 9- Applying water from the right side, 10- Wiping the inner and outer ears with water, 11- The second and third washing of the face — each of these is a Sunnah, while the first is obligatory, 12- And the siwak. We say that the greater purity is the washing of the entire body.

33


<!-- page 38 -->
The obligatory ghusl (ritual bath) is divided into seven types, and the recommended ghusl into twelve. The obligatory types are: 1- Ghusl from janabah (major ritual impurity); 2- Ghusl following menstruation; 3- Ghusl upon a disbeliever's conversion to Islam; 4- Ghusl for one who loses consciousness upon regaining it; 5- Ghusl following nifas (postnatal bleeding); 6- Ghusl after contact with ritual impurity; 7- Washing the deceased, which is a communal obligation upon the living until someone fulfills it. The recommended types are: 1- Friday ghusl; 2- Ghusl for Eid al-Fitr; 3- Ghusl for Eid al-Adha; 4- Ghusl for entering the state of ihram; 5- Ghusl upon entering the Haram; 6- Ghusl upon entering the Ka'bah; 7- Ghusl upon entering Madinah; 8- Ghusl on the day of Arafah; 9- Ghusl on three nights of Ramadan: the nineteenth; 10- the twenty-first; 11- the twenty-third; 12- and ghusl for washing the deceased.

Mawlana Amir al-Mu'minin Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him, said in his Four Hundred Etiquettes: "Cleanse yourself with water of foul-smelling waste." He also said therein: "When one of you wishes to relieve himself, let him say: In the name of God — O God, remove harm from me and keep the devil away from my body. And when he is done, let him say: Praise be to God, who fed me in health and expelled its harm from me in health."

He also said: "When one of you looks upon what he has expelled after finishing, let him say: O God, grant me what is lawful and spare me what is forbidden — for the Messenger of God, may God's peace and blessings be upon him, said: There is no servant but that God has assigned to him an angel who cranes its neck to observe him whenever he relieves himself and looks at what he has produced. The angel then asks God to grant him what is lawful, and the angel says: O son of Adam, this is what you took care to preserve —

<!-- page 39 -->
so consider where you took it from and what became of it." He also said: "If sleep touches the heart, wudhu' becomes obligatory."

**On the Etiquette of Wudhu'**

In the book of questions and answers — arranged according to the chapters of the Pillars of Islam — he said: Question: What does our master say regarding one who forgot to seek God's assistance before relieving himself of a major impurity, and then prayed until the prayer time had passed before remembering — may he perform wudhu' and repeat the prayer or not? Answer: He must repeat the prayer, because one does not pray while in a state of impurity.

Question: What does our master Adam — may God support him — say concerning a man who performs wudhu' and washes in a washing cloth while naked, in a place where someone might see him — should he pray with that wudhu' or not? Answer: Some of the Imams, peace be upon them, have indicated that being immersed in water is preferable from the standpoint of good conduct. If a person is not seen by anyone, being naked carries no objection, and it is preferable that he pray with the purity he has already performed even while naked. As for those who take care regarding a place in which one should not relieve oneself, one should not return to that very stone but should move away from it after relieving oneself. From the Book of Clarification: The Messenger of God, may God's peace and blessings be upon him, said: Gabriel, peace be upon him, commanded me on behalf of my Lord to wash and launder my clothes when performing wudhu'. And from an authentic

<!-- page 40 -->
hadith: "When you perform wudhu', do not neglect the finkh" — meaning the side of the tunnel on the right and on the left, which is the hollow. He said in a book: Whoever forgets his wudhu' until he completes the wash carries no obligation, but if a man begins wudhu' and then performs ghusl without completing the ghusl, he should carry on with the ghusl and perform wudhu' afterward for prayer.

He said in the book Ta'wil al-Da'a'im: The one who intends to perform wudhu' should make the vessel containing the water for his wudhu' and purification visible before him, taking it with his right hand. He also said in the first mentioned book of questions and answers, arranged according to the chapters of the Pillars of Islam — Question: What does our master say — may God continue to support him — regarding one who entered prayer having performed wudhu', then recalled that he forgot the invocation: does he complete the prayer or repeat it? Answer: It is not permissible for a man to pray while knowing of an impurity until he has washed it away.

He also said in the Book of the Interpretation of the Shari'ah, in the section on the etiquette of wudhu': The etiquette of wudhu' consists of twelve points: 1- Do not enter the open space except wearing sandals; 2- Advance your left foot when entering; 3- Cover your head until you exit; 4- Do not face the qiblah nor turn your back to it; 5- Cover yourself; 6- Lean to your left side; 7- Do not linger in the open space; 8- Do not seek to relieve yourself on a smeared surface, a branded place, dung, or soft earth — use three clean stones instead; 9- Cleanse yourself with your left hand; 10- Do not pour water on the excrement; 11- Do not speak until you exit; 12- Advance your right foot as you leave. The excrement referred to is

<!-- page 41 -->
human waste, branded surfaces, and worn bones. He said: "It is not permissible to pray without wudhu'." The Prophet, may God's peace and blessings be upon him, said: "Do not urinate in burrows," adding that they are the shelter of snakes and serpents. The Prophet, may God's peace and blessings be upon him and his family, also said: "Prayer is not accepted without purity." And Mawlana Amir al-Mu'minin Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him, said in his Four Hundred Etiquettes: "Rinsing the mouth and inhaling water into the nose during purification cleanses the mouth and nose, and is Sunnah." He said: "Seeking purification with cold water cuts off hemorrhoids." He also said: "Wudhu' after purification earns ten good deeds, so let them purify themselves." He said: "A man should not perform wudhu' without saying, before the water touches him: In the name of God — O God, make me among the repentant and among the purified." He said: "When a man is naked, the devil looks at him and covets him, so let them conceal themselves." He said: "It is not permissible for a man to uncover himself and sit in the presence of others." He said: "Wash the hands of your children after eating, for the devil smells food remnants and the child is frightened in his sleep and the scribes are harmed."

**On the Characteristics of Wudhu'**

He said in the first mentioned book of questions and answers, arranged according to the chapters of the Pillars of Islam — Question: What do you say, O best of those

<!-- page 42 -->
whose love we seek in wisdom and among the wise, regarding what is stated in the book of purity — that rinsing the mouth, inhaling and exhaling water is a Sunnah that should not be deliberately abandoned, and that forgetting it or neglecting it does not invalidate one's wudhu', nor does it require repetition if one has already prayed — what is meant by this invocation? Answer: The invocation, mouth-rinsing, and nasal inhalation are Sunnah practices that should not be deliberately abandoned. Whoever neglects them does not thereby invalidate his prayer. The obligation is to purify away ritual impurity, since removing impurity is itself obligatory.

Question: What do you say — O best of human beings, of auspicious human beings, beloved of parents and loved ones — regarding what is mentioned in the book of the Pillars of Islam concerning the command to wash the face while drinking from the eyes: does the omission of wudhu' apply to one who closes his eyes while washing his face, or not? Answer: If he wets his eyes it is preferable, but if he does not, his wudhu' is still valid.

Question: What do you say — O one for whom God is not pleased except through His righteous servants — regarding a man who has wounds or boils on his face or hands that water cannot touch: should he leave those areas unwashed during wudhu' or ghusl? Answer: Whoever has on his limbs or elsewhere a wound or boil that water cannot touch may wipe over the dressings and coverings. The same applies to wounds even if he fears that removing the dressings will cause harm through cold or severe injury. This is the ruling applicable to that condition in wudhu'. In the case of ghusl, if there is an ailment such as smallpox, wounds, or

<!-- page 43 -->
the like, water is poured over them in general; if he fears harm to himself, he should perform tayammum, whether he is travelling or at home.

Question: What do Mawlana and Mawlana — may God grant us His pleasure — say regarding wiping over the two legs when the upper part is exposed and the lower part is beneath: is it permissible to wipe over the sole or not? Answer: The sole is an obligatory part over which wiping is required.

Question: What do you say — may God continue to support you — regarding one who entered prayer after performing wudhu', then recalled that he had forgotten the water: may he pray as is, or must he repeat? Answer: It is not permissible for a person who knows of an impurity to pray until he has washed it away. As for those who perform wudhu' and forget to wash their hands and face, and then pray, with the time having passed and the recollection coming only afterward — they do not need to repeat the prayer. And if a man performs wudhu' and then rises and walks, and the limbs dry out from the beginning of the wudhu' before he completes it, he must start over; but if they have not dried out, he may continue with the remainder. Whoever performs wudhu' for prayer, then rises and sees afterward that a spot from the wudhu' has dried, does not repeat the wudhu' — he simply wipes the dry spot. As for a man with broken hands who cannot perform wudhu' for prayer, his slave girl who belongs to him performs it for him; if he has no such person, and his wife is unavailable, he completes it as best he can and prays.

Question: What do you say — O one whom God Almighty has made trustee of His guardians — regarding a ghadeer or a place without water, where people relieve themselves, and then water flows into it carrying filth, and it collects until it rises above the threshold that overpowers impurities, with none of the characteristics of impurity present: is it permissible to purify oneself with it or not? Answer: If the water overwhelms the impurity, purification with it is permissible provided it is well above the threshold stipulated in the Shari'ah.

<!-- page 44 -->
Question: What do you say — O one whom God has made the apple of the eye of the beloved of God — regarding a vessel filled with water beyond the threshold, into which an impurity then appears or falls, causing one of its characteristics to manifest, after which more water is poured in until the trace of the impurity is removed: is it permissible to purify oneself with it or not? Answer: Water that does not become impure is permissible for purification, provided it is above the threshold after the vessel has been purified.

Question: What do you say — O one whom God Almighty has made a spring of water by which the souls of the people of Islam are sustained — regarding a small amount of pure water with which a person purifies himself while carrying no impurity on his body, after which another purifies himself with it, and then another, and likewise with the dust used in tayammum, given that the Shari'ah states that used water is pure and cleansing, while no mention of used soil is found therein — does the general principle not permit using water and earth for purification more than once? Answer: It is permissible to purify oneself with such water repeatedly.

Question: What do you say regarding the meaning of the saying of the Prophet, peace be upon him: "Whoever does not purify himself with the sea, God will not purify him"? Answer: Water purifies but is not itself purified by anything else — and likewise the knowledge of the Imam: whoever does not draw purification from him will not be purified by anything else. Even if the water in a pool is eight cubits in length and width, though its depth is measured in spans or less, purification with it is permissible. If rain falls upon a city and water flows — both clean and dirty — into a large pool in which a man and a camel could be submerged, and the pool fills up, washing and purifying with it is permissible, unless its color, taste, or smell changes. And if water is drawn from a well with buckets, the water runs, and then a non-Muslim washes a garment in it and it is seen to be dirty —

<!-- page 45 -->
purification with it is permissible, as the ruling follows that of a slave. From the book of the Summary of Works: if a man finds a small amount of water on the ground, in a hollow, or in something similar, and it is significantly altered, and he does not know whether it has been mixed with impurity or not — for water may change without any admixture of impurity — then what has been extracted from plant materials by pressing or distillation, such as rosewater and the like, may not be used for purification. And what is used to purify the limbs without the impurity having been on them, not having collected in a vessel, is not impure, and purification with it is permissible. It is also permissible to perform wudhu' with water that has been used once for wudhu' without being mixed with impurity.

He mentioned in the Supporting Councils: "One should benefit from rainwater at the time of rain." He also said in the book Mukhtasar al-Musannaf: whoever has on his hand a ring bearing the name of God should not begin the purification ritual while in a state of major ritual impurity. Our master Qadi al-Nu'man ibn Muhammad — may God exalt him — said in the Book of Clarification, in the section on the description of washing the hands: what was narrated from Abu Abdillah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, includes the instruction that one should not wipe the arms from the elbow upward, and that wiping is obligatory for the hands because of the absence of hair on them — as is likewise the case for the head. It was also stated that if one performing wudhu' interrupts it, he may continue from where he left off, unless the water on the limbs he has already washed has dried. He also said in the book Ta'wil al-Shari'ah of Mawlana al-Mu'izz li-Din Allah — may God's prayers be upon him — that the Prophet, may God's peace and blessings be upon him, said: "If a dog laps from the vessel of one of you, let him wash it seven times, the last of them with dirt." And he said in


<!-- page 46 -->
In short, if a vessel becomes impure from contact with a dog, it must be washed seven times — the first time with dirt. The pig is treated the same. It was also stated that if two vessels contain impurity and one does not know which of them it is, he should not perform ablution with either of them; instead, he should perform tayammum and pray, and no sin falls upon him.

In the Majlis al-Bayan it is stated that the obligatory elements of ablution are seven: 1 – Pure water; 2 – Intention; 3 – Washing the face; 4 – Washing the hands; 5 – Wiping the head; 6 – The two feet; 7 – Performing these in the order in which Allah mentioned them. The Sunnah acts are twelve: 1 – Istiqsa' (thoroughness); 2 – Washing the hands before inserting them into the vessel; 3 – Pronouncing the name of Allah when sitting down for ablution; 4 – Rinsing the mouth; 5 – Inhaling water into the nostrils; 6 – Moving the ring; 7 – Combing through the beard; 8 – Moistening the eyes; 9 – Pouring water from the steps; 10 – Wiping the ears; 11 – Performing each act three times — the first wash being obligatory, the second and third being Sunnah; 12 – Using the siwak.

The obligatory forms of ghusl are seven: 1 – Ghusl from janabah; 2 – Ghusl from menstruation; 3 – Ghusl of the disbeliever upon entering Islam; 4 – Ghusl of the one who recovers from madness or fainting; 5 – Ghusl from the blood of nifas; 6 – Ghusl from contact with impurity; 7 – Washing the deceased, which is a collective obligation upon the living until someone fulfils it.

The Sunnah forms of ghusl are twelve: 1 – Friday ghusl; 2–3 – Ghusl for the two Eids; 4 – Ghusl for ihram; 5 – Ghusl upon entering the Haram; 6 – Ghusl upon entering

<!-- page 47 -->
the Ka'bah; 7 – Ghusl upon entering Madinah; 8 – Ghusl on the day of Arafah; 9 – Ghusl on three nights of Ramadan — the nineteenth night; 10 – The twenty-first night; 11 – The twenty-third night; 12 – Ghusl as one of the washings of the deceased.

Prayer is disliked in twelve places: 1 – The cemetery; 2 – The slaughterhouse; 3 – Pigeon coops; 4 – Rubbish heaps; 5 – Camel enclosures; 6 – Praying while drowsy; 7 – Praying without an upper garment; 8 – On wet, impure ground; 9 – On food; 10 – In Jewish synagogues; 11 – In Christian churches; 12 – In Zoroastrian places of worship — unless water has been sprinkled over them.

It is stated in the book of the interpretation of the Shari'ah that nothing but purity is to be sought for the purpose of ritual, and that this requires a tranquil heart and soul, along with certainty that purity has been achieved and impurity removed. It is also stated there that the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) forbade wiping over socks and abandoning the basmalah in public. Our master Ja'far ibn Mansur al-Yaman, may Allah sanctify him, said in the book of inner spiritual instruction that the Sunnah in ablution is to perform each act three times in every place of ablution, except for the private parts — there one washes until the impurity is removed. He then said, may Allah sanctify him, that ablution is thus threefold, and he said thereafter that the Sunnah in ghusl is for a person to wash with water until it drips and flows freely. If he cannot find sufficient water, he should wipe as much of his body as the water reaches. In the summary of transmitted accounts, in a discussion of the characteristics of ablution, it is stated that one rinses the mouth and inhales water three times — both being part of the Sunnah and not to be abandoned. If one omits them unintentionally, nothing is required of him.

<!-- page 48 -->
He washes each place three times; if he washes twice or once, he has left something incomplete — three times is better, and no more than three. It is stated there that one should be rewarded for that once, and if he wiped three times that is good. Our master the long-serving preacher Zayd ibn Muhammad ibn al-Fadl, may Allah sanctify his soul, said in the hidden message: Allah Most High said — and He is the truest of those who speak — "Indeed, Allah loves the repentant and loves those who purify themselves."
