The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, said: "No prayer is accepted except with purity." Purity is of two kinds, and filth is of two kinds. Outward purity is the cleansing with visible water from outward filth — urine, faeces, and the like.

<!-- page 49 -->
Inner purity is achieved through inner water, which is knowledge, cleansing one from inner filth, which is doubt and ignorance.

He said further that ablution encompasses seven meanings, four obligatory and three Sunnah, and that the Prophet, peace be upon him and his family, placed the three Sunnah acts before the four obligatory ones. He mentioned washing the hands before inserting them into the vessel, and said — because the mouth is nourished by the various foods and drinks in a way that no other human sense is — that it is the passage through which the five senses are fed and through which their outward manifestation occurs in a way unlike any other. He then said: the mouth is to the lesser world as the intellect is to the greater; whoever seeks nourishment without a mouth cannot be fed and cannot live. As for inhaling through the nose, he said it is the organ through which the body draws breath from the air, so that the body lives at rest; when the nostrils are blocked, a person is compelled to breathe through the mouth in their place, and when both the nose and mouth are blocked, a person is damaged and cannot live. He then said that rinsing and inhaling

<!-- page 49 -->
and istiqsa' are three Sunnah acts, while washing the face, washing the hands, wiping the head, and wiping the feet are obligatory — making seven limits in all. One of the two ghusl types is bounded and the other is unbounded; one of the two acts of anointing is bounded and the other is not. The face, in the sense of the speaking self, is so named because it holds the seven openings — the eyes, the ears, the nostrils, and the mouth — and a person knows his own face as he knows nothing else; there is no salvation except by turning toward it. Maulana the Commander of the Faithful Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him, stated in his Four Hundred Precepts that abstaining from intoxicants and wiping over socks are matters of taqiyyah.

**Mentioning ghusl from janabah**

In the aforementioned question-and-answer book, arranged according to the chapters of the book The Pillars of Islam, it is asked: What do you say, O one whose praise and thanks surpass what our tongues can express, regarding what is mentioned in some books of Shari'ah — may Allah's peace be upon its owner — that if a person fears the cold and is in a state requiring ghusl, he should perform tayammum and pray, and then if he becomes able to use water, wash and repeat the prayer? Does this ruling apply to both the resident and the traveller? Answer:

<!-- page 50 -->
If he fears that water will harm him — whether he is a resident or a traveller, whether near or far — he should perform tayammum and pray.

Question: What do you say, O one confirmed in his highest and finest merit, regarding ghusl from janabah when intercourse occurred, for example, for the sake of benefit and advantage, and a similar ghusl is required — as the washer is required to mention when performing ghusl, since the relevant texts in the books of the guiding Da'wah are extensive? Answer: Ghusl is like the removal of doubt and uncertainty, which only occurs when clarification and benefit are given.

Question: What do you say, O one whom Allah has made an extended rope from heaven to the earth for the people of this world who remember Allah in both states of reproach and praise — it is stated in the Shari'ah that if a person faints for several days, or if it becomes known that he is perhaps in a state of janabah, then ghusl is required; what is the threshold for this if no trace of semen is visible? Answer: If he does not know his own state, ghusl is still obligatory if he has doubt about it.

Question: What does our master say, may Allah inspire him with righteous guidance — regarding a weak man afflicted by janabah who fears the cold if he washes, and the prayer time has come: may he perform tayammum as a sick person would, or not? Answer: If he fears water, he should perform tayammum and pray, and he need not repeat it.

Question: What does our master say, may Allah perpetuate His support of him, regarding a man who wakes from sleep and sees in his garment the trace of semen — wet or dry — but does not recall having seen in his dream that he had experienced a discharge: must he perform ghusl or not? Answer: He must perform ghusl.

Question: What do you say, O one to whom questioners look for the fulfilment of their needs — it is mentioned in the Shari'ah that it is disliked for a person in a state of janabah

<!-- page 51 -->
or a menstruating woman to cut hair or nails, and the soul remains in such a state during menstruation — is it permissible for them to do so out of necessity or not? Answer: He should leave it until he becomes pure.

Question: What do you say, O one through whom the pure seeker has arrived — is it permissible for a person in a state of janabah or a menstruating woman to recite supplications or other books that contain material which the reader recognizes or does not recognize as Quranic? How should such a person handle the reading or the leaving of it, given that the Shari'ah states they should not recite our Quran and should not touch it? And may a person in a state of janabah or a menstruating woman recite takbir, praise, and glorification — expressions that all appear in the Quran — and may such a person recite the basmalah, given that it has been said to be a verse of the Quran? Answer: Neither the menstruating woman nor the person in a state of janabah may recite any part of the Quran — whether in prayer or in books — deliberately, and neither may recite the basmalah with the intention of reciting the Quran.

It is stated in a book that if a man forgets that he is in a state requiring ablution until he has performed ghusl, the ghusl suffices and he need not repeat the ablution — rather he should complete his ghusl as ghusl, after which ablution for prayer has been fulfilled.

The answer given by some of the preachers of Yemen to some scholars from the Indian lands, may Allah be pleased with them: if a person in a state of janabah and a deceased person are together and there is only enough water for one of them, the person in a state of janabah should perform ghusl and the deceased should have tayammum performed for him. The owner of the hidden message said, may Allah sanctify his soul: Allah has enjoined ghusl from janabah, and said: "If you are in a state of janabah, then purify yourselves." The Prophet, peace be upon him and his family, said: "Beneath every hair of janabah" — meaning that janabah reaches to the roots of the hair — "so purify yourselves." He then said: whoever does not do so is negligent and far from

<!-- page 52 -->
that which he claims, meaning far from his proper lineage, for obeying the state of janabah is to fall into ruin and falsehood. The Commander of the Faithful, Maulana Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him, said in his Four Hundred Precepts: a Muslim should not sleep while in a state of janabah, and should not sleep unless he is purified. If he finds no water, he should perform tayammum, for the soul of the believer ascends to Allah Almighty, who receives it and blesses it. If He has prepared a place for it, He places it in the finest form; and if its appointed time has not yet arrived, He sends it back to its body with His guardian angels.

**Mentioning the purity of bodies, garments, the earth, carpets, and the like**

In the aforementioned question-and-answer book, arranged according to the chapters of The Pillars of Islam: Question: What do you say, O best of those whom Allah has commanded us to rely upon for judgment according to Allah's decree — regarding vomit: does it render impure whatever it falls upon, as blood, pus, and other impurities do, or not? Answer: Vomit does render impure whatever it falls upon, and the first thing to be done is to wash the affected area.

Question: What do you say, O best of those whom Allah Almighty has placed as a guide for those who seek to reach Allah Almighty — regarding land on which children have urinated and defecated, which has then dried up and from which the smell of the impurity and its physical trace have disappeared: is it permissible to pray on it without further washing, or not? Answer: If the physical trace of the impurity has been removed, it is treated as pure land, provided it is not

<!-- page 53 -->
a passage used for need. Question: What do you say, O one whom Allah Almighty has made a criterion by which the corrupt are distinguished from the pure in the religion of Allah — it is stated in the Shari'ah that if land is struck by impurity and then water flows over it and it dries up and the smell and trace of the impurity disappear, prayer on it is permitted; and it is also stated that it is permissible to pray in dry places where camels and sheep are penned: O our master, what is the distinction between the two cases? Answer: If sunlight and wind have purified it, and the physical trace and smell of the impurity have been removed from it, then prayer on it is permissible and valid.

Question: What does our master say, may Allah preserve the seat of his authority — regarding the meaning of the statement of the Commander of the Faithful that whoever touches a dead body after it has cooled must perform ghusl: what is the significance of "after it has cooled"? Answer: "After it has cooled" means after it has become impure; when it infects what is adjacent to it through its moisture, that adjacent thing becomes impure — and this is the meaning of the obligation to wash. It is Sunnah that this washing and the washing from contact with the deceased body be performed, except when the body has already been washed by bathing.

Question: What about something that a person wants to purify but cannot wring out or squeeze three times — such as the scalp, mats, rugs, mosque quilts, and mattresses: what is the proper method for purifying them? Answer: For something that one wishes to purify but cannot wring out or squeeze three times, one should run his hand over it until the impurity and its smell are gone, then lift it from the water and leave it until it stops dripping, then repeat this three times, and so on.


<!-- page 54 -->
He should tread on it with his feet as described, then remove it from the water and press it until the water comes out, then return it three times. Question: Does a breastfeeding woman's milk that falls on another person's clothes make those clothes impure? Answer: By the grace of God, al-Ma'ad ibn Abdullah ibn Ali — may God sanctify him — held that the milk of a Muslim breastfeeding woman is pure, both for her and for others. This is a sound position, because her milk is analogous to her saliva, and the saliva of every Muslim is pure and does not make impure whatever it touches. Question: If a man shaves his head while wet and some of his hair falls onto his garment or body, does that hair make what it touches impure? Answer: In the easier position, this is permitted in cases of impurity. Question: Do the milk, fat, and eggs of jallalah animals — cows, camels, sheep, and chickens — make the clothes or bodies of worshippers impure if they come into contact with them? Answer: Based on what was stated in the first issue, a small amount of this is permissible. Question: Must a traveler or sick person who cannot clean their impure clothes perform those prayers later as make-up prayers? Answer: Prayer should not be performed except with a pure garment, as noted when the topic was addressed, and the impurity of garments was not separately mentioned. As for the sick person, he should instruct those around him to wash his clothes. Question: A polytheist who worships idols and does not know what is lawful or unlawful weaves cloth, cuts it to size, and then sews it — is the finished garment pure, and is it permissible to pray in it? God said:

<!-- page 55 -->
"The polytheists are impure." Answer: All garments are pure until they are worn while impure or until some impurity appears on them, as he stated in the book of evidences. The Imams permitted prayer in garments made by polytheists unless those polytheists have worn them or impurity is visibly present in them. He also stated — may God sustain him — that a garment dyed by a polytheist is pure even if he wore it, but if he wore it while impure, it becomes impure. Question: What does our master — may God bless his support — say about garments that polytheists have cut but not yet worn: can they be purified, and if so, how? Answer: By the grace of God and drawing from the blessings of His saints, I hold that this cannot purify the clothes of polytheists that they produced before wearing them; but as for what they cut and washed, it is not permissible to consider them pure except by washing them. Those who heard this statement did not grasp the root of the question. Question: What does our master — may God perpetuate his support — say about garments made by polytheists that they have not worn, but that were splashed with impure water during the weaving and stitching process, and the water then dried? When such garments are sold and bought, and the splashing has dried, is it permissible to pray in them? Answer: There is nothing wrong with praying in such garments; it is permissible to do so. Question: What do you say — you who have become the refuge of those who seek your guidance, who take pride in your biography — regarding a wide, long floor-covering that is partly pure and partly impure: is it permissible to pray on the purified portion? The common people say that if a garment has been stirred as much as

<!-- page 56 -->
one part of it without the other end moving, is it permissible to pray on the purified portion? Answer: It is not permissible to pray on it. He stated in the book of summaries that praying in the clothes of boys who are prone to impurity is permissible unless impurity in them is known, and that a small amount of discharge is excusable. He also stated regarding innocent houses that one should not stop praying in them, though it is not recommended. He stated in the book of questions and answers — the questions of which were posed by the late Sheikh Yusuf ibn Ismail ibn Ibrahim al-Samti al-Hindi in his letters to our master Idris ibn al-Hasan — may God Most High sanctify him. Question: Is it permissible to pray on stones or plaster? Please give the correct answer. Answer: The Prophet — peace and blessings be upon him and his family — said: "The earth has been made for me a mosque and a means of purification," and anything over which tayammum is not permissible cannot be prayed upon from the ground. Question: What do you say about sweeping houses? Most people in India do not sweep at night at all, and they also avoid sweeping shops and marketplaces at night. How should we understand this? May God grant us guidance. Answer: This view is not reliable, and there is nothing wrong with one who does it. He stated in the book of the interpretation of the Sharia that the Prophet — peace and blessings be upon him — said: "If a dog licks the vessel of one of you, wash it seven times, one of them with dirt." He stated in the summary that if a vessel becomes impure from a dog's contact, it should be washed seven times, the first of them with dirt, and that a pig is treated the same. He also stated that if one of two vessels contains impurity and it is not known which one,

<!-- page 57 -->
one should not approach either of them and should pray. The Commander of the Believers, Ali ibn Abi Talib — peace be upon him — said in his four hundred etiquettes: trimming the mustache is clean and is a Sunnah; it is said that washing the head with sidr removes illness and is from the Sunnah and removes filth. He said in it that light is a tonic for the body and a purification for it, and that trimming the nails wards off the gravest of diseases, brings sustenance, and brings increase. He said in it that plucking the armpits removes offensive odor and is a purification that is from the Sunnah. He said in it that washing on feast days is a purification for one who seeks his needs before God Almighty and follows the Sunnah. He said in it that washing clothes removes worry and suppresses the enemy, and it is a purification for prayer. He said in it: do not shorten the mustache, for it is a light for the Muslim. He said in it: a young man in appearance but old in Islam shall have a light on the Day of Resurrection. He said in it: let none of you relieve himself on the argument — meaning the road — nor urinate into still water; whoever does so and something befalls him has only himself to blame, for the road is shared and the water is shared. Let none of you be careless: do not urinate in a way that splashes back on him, and do not let urine come back on him. He said in it: do not let any of you relieve himself on the road or on his face. Whoever you see sleeping on his face, warn him. He said in it: purify yourself with water from foul-smelling wind and commit to this, for God Almighty hates the filthy servant who repels those who sit with him. He said: walk away from dogs. If a dry dog has touched his garment, let him sprinkle water on it; if the dog was wet, he should wash it.

<!-- page 58 -->
He said in it that it is preferable for a believer to apply oil every fifteen days. He stated in the book of questions and answers — from the late Sheikh Shimon ibn Muhammad Ghori the Indian, may God be pleased with him — with the answers from our master Badr al-Din, our master Hasan ibn Idris — may God sanctify him and grant us his intercession. Question: What do you say, O one through whom our souls live, regarding a man who lets the hair of his head grow and does not part it every day: does this constitute a deficiency in his prayers or not? And how often should he comb and part it? Answer: If it is in two halves, he should not leave it without parting it; if he did so deliberately, the ruling invalidates his prayer. He said: What do you say regarding the meaning of the statement of Ali — peace be upon him — "Whoever does not purify the sea, God will not purify him"? Answer: Just as water purifies and is not itself purified, so too is the knowledge of the Imam: whoever does not purify it, God does not purify him.

Mention of Siwak

He stated in the first-mentioned book of questions and answers, which is arranged according to the order of the chapters of the book "The Pillars of Islam." Question: What do you say — O you who grant our repentant souls relief upon hearing your words — regarding the practice of most people in India, who, after finishing the siwak, take a piece of oud, bamboo, or some other material and use it with their tongues to remove dirt: is this permissible or

<!-- page 59 -->
not? Answer: The siwak is the better practice, but there is nothing wrong with doing that. Others say: one should not disturb the breach that has a knot of hashish. Others say: it is disliked to seek it at Uhud while one is in a state of janaba. From the book of explaining the news: basil is every plant with a pleasant scent, and myrtle was singled out for this distinction. From the book of the interpretation of the pillars: basil in the tongue means the tips of every plant that is good when the wind passes over it, as light basil is the name for the mosque of pleasant breezes, but the scholars were most concerned with myrtle — that one should keep it and hold it close. The Commander of the Believers, Ali ibn Abi Talib — peace be upon him — said in his four hundred etiquettes: the siwak is pleasing to God Almighty and good for the mouth, and it is from the Sunnah.

Remembrance of Tayammum

He stated in the first-mentioned book of questions and answers, which is arranged according to the order of the chapters of the book "The Pillars of Islam." Question: What do you say — O you who urge us better than any other to sit with the scholars in the constant tranquility of the earth — is tayammum permissible on a flat stone even if there is no dust on it? And if tayammum on it is not permissible unless there is dust on it, what then is the difference between it and other stones? Answer: If there is dust on it, tayammum is valid; if there is no dust on it, tayammum is not valid. Question: What does our master — may God perpetuate his support — say regarding a man who travels from a

<!-- page 60 -->
city to a place within the travel limit, and the time for prayer arrives during the journey and he finds no water: is it permissible for him to perform tayammum in this situation? Answer: It is not permissible for him to do so except if he is far from water while traveling and cannot perform ablution until he misses the prayer time, or if he fears for himself or his property if he goes to the water — then he may perform tayammum. Question: Regarding a man who needs to perform ghusl on the night of Ramadan and forgets about ghusl until he departs on a journey: does performing tayammum earn him the reward for his prayer and fasting or not? Answer: If he forgets to perform ghusl until he travels and does not find water, he is complete in his prayer and fasting. Question: What about a man who dies while traveling and there is no water? Answer: In that case, tayammum is performed over him. Others say: whoever was traveling and the prayer time came and water was present — if he fears for himself due to illness, he completes his prayer. Question: Regarding a traveler who has with him a small amount of water sufficient only for ablution of the limbs — which should he begin with? And if he does not deduce, must he perform ghusl or not? Answer: Our master al-Nu'man — may God sanctify his soul — said in the book of purity: if he is saved by stones or by something else, the purest available thing serves as ablution when there is a cause, and the absence of water is a cause and the meaning of tayammum. Question: Regarding a man who is present and whose private parts contain a reason that prevents him from facing the qibla: if he washes his limbs, must he then perform tayammum after that or not? Answer: This has already been addressed in the answer to the first question. Question:

<!-- page 61 -->
Regarding the traveler who prays obligatory prayers in full before their time has ended — if he misses them, when is he obligated to make them up in full? Answer: If he has no water, it is permissible to pray by performing tayammum and to make them up afterward. Similarly, if a female traveler menstruates and then becomes pure and finds no water, she has fulfilled her prayers and it becomes lawful for her husband to have intercourse with her. If a group of people are traveling and have an imam among them, and the water they have is only enough for one man's ablution, he should perform tayammum in front of them and lead them in prayer. If a man is on a journey and has with him only enough water for his ablution, and there is impurity on his garment, he should wash his garment with that water, then perform tayammum and pray. If a man avoids traveling and is present for the noon prayer, and he has water sufficient only for ablution but not for ghusl, he should perform tayammum and pray. If he is present for the afternoon prayer and that water is still with him, he should use it for ablution and need not repeat the tayammum, because he is already purified from janaba. If a deceased person and one in a state of janaba meet and together do not have enough water for a single complete wash, the water should be used to wash the deceased. If a man travels with a Christian or Zoroastrian woman and she menstruates on the way, then becomes pure, and finds no water, the husband should teach her how to perform the complete tayammum and she acts accordingly. He stated in the book of summaries of practice: if a group of people are traveling and have an imam among them and their water is sufficient only for one man's ablution, he should lead them in prayer after they all perform tayammum. If a man is on a journey and has only enough water for his ablution and is in a state of


<!-- page 62 -->
If his garment is impure, he should wash it with that water and then pray. If a man is traveling and the time of the noon prayer arrives while he has only enough water for ablution — not enough for a full ritual bath — he should pray without performing the bath. Then, if the time of the 'Asr prayer arrives and that water is still with him, he performs ablution with it, having not used it before because he was in a state of major ritual impurity. If water is found after he has completed his dry ablution (tayammum), he returns to using water only if he has not yet washed and the prayer time remains. If the Maghrib prayer time arrives and he has water sufficient only for tayammum, he performs tayammum. If the 'Isha' prayer time arrives and that water is still with him, he uses it for ablution, since he is in a state of minor impurity at that point. If a non-believer embraces Islam and cannot find water, he prays as he is. It is also recommended that a man who sleeps in the Prophet's mosque while in a state of major ritual impurity, and who wakes to find himself still in that state, should not pass through the Sacred Mosque until he has exited it — and the same applies within the Haram. If the obligations of washing the deceased and performing the ritual bath coincide and there is only enough water for one, the bath for the living takes precedence over washing the deceased. As for the sick person who cannot perform tayammum, the correct position from the book mentioned is that a man in a state of major impurity in Egypt may perform tayammum to pray over a deceased person if he fears missing the funeral prayer. Whoever prays with tayammum while traveling and then finds water should, upon remembering and while the prayer time remains, prefer to perform ablution and repeat the prayer — even if the time has passed, he is rewarded for what he did and may pray again. It is stated in the Muntakhab:

All who could not find soil —
let them shake their garments and clothes.

<!-- page 63 -->
Even if nothing visible clings to them, whatever dust may be present suffices for the purpose.

In the book of Ta'wil al-Da'im, it is stated: a man pressed by a crowd who cannot reach water and whose prayer time arrives should perform tayammum and pray, then repeat that prayer — meaning if he is able to find water afterward and purify himself with it. It is also said in a book on questions and answers that if a woman purifies herself from menstrual blood during the month of Ramadan before the time of Maghrib, and she fears harm from performing the ritual bath, she may perform tayammum, keep her fast, and need not make up that day. The same applies to a man who has a nocturnal emission during Ramadan: if performing the ritual bath at night would endanger his health, he performs tayammum and fasts, with no obligation to make up that fast. In a book containing comprehensive legal questions on a topic beyond the scope of another work, the following question is posed: two men prayed in one place because they could not find water, yet the prayer of one was valid while the prayer of the other was not — how is this so? The answer: one man said to his companion, "Teach me tayammum," and the other taught him and struck the ground on his behalf. Both prayed; the prayer of the one who learned was valid, but the prayer of the teacher was not, because he did not form the intention for himself.

**On the Purity of Foods and Drinks**

In the first-mentioned book of questions and answers, arranged according to the order of the chapters of the book on the Pillars of Islam, the following question is posed: What do you say — O one whom Allah Almighty has made a fortress, from whose door those who have been cursed seek refuge — regarding the oil known as Nimi

<!-- page 64 -->
that people know is eaten only by crows, whose pits then pass out through their droppings and dry in the sun, and which people then collect, sell, and purchase? These pits are pressed and what comes out of them as an oil is sold, and one does not detect any impurity in it. Is it permissible to sell it, buy it, handle it, and touch it, or not? Answer: Whatever is not permissible to eat is impure, and what is impure may not be eaten, sold, or bought.

Another question: O best of our sight, from the stock of his knowledge — our eyes have not encountered in the book of purity any statement objecting to the touch of a menstruating woman, a Jew, a Christian, a Sabian, or animals. Yet it is also stated therein that the dog is impure. How are these two narrations reconciled? Answer: If there is moisture involved, the thing becomes impure by contact with what is impure — though the impurity does not arise merely from handling it dry, just as with the skin of a dead animal: selling it is not permissible, while the first ruling applies to water above the threshold. Question: What does our master Malik — may Allah perpetuate his support — say regarding honey, clarified butter (ghee), oil, and liquid fat in which a mouse has died? Should it be fed to animals or buried in the ground? And if soap is made from it — placed on a mat (saji) — may it be sold in the market or given to people without charge? Answer: When an animal dies in liquid substances, those liquids may not be eaten and may not be fed to a carnivorous animal, for that would be forbidden. It is, however, permissible to make soap from it, though the soap may not be sold unless the buyer is informed of its impurity — and the garment washed with that impure soap must be rinsed again with pure water afterward. And

<!-- page 65 -->
from the book of reports: it may be fed to dogs and to animals whose meat is not eaten. From this also: if a dead mouse is found in a pot of broth, the meat should be washed and may be eaten, along with other food from which a single grain of the mouse's droppings appears — it is removed and the food is eaten. The same applies if two grains appear; but if three grains appear, a person may not eat it. As for an animal that has drunk impure liquid — is its milk still permissible to consume? Question: What do you say regarding a squirrel that is eaten and in which there is a small amount of something — is it permissible to eat it or not? Answer: If the foreign matter is something small that causes no harm to a person, it is permissible to eat it along with the rest. Similarly, if there is something small from the dust in the water of a vessel, it is not forbidden unless it is something foul that would cause harm upon consumption. Allah and His guardians know best. Question: What do you say regarding the clarified butter, oil, sugar, honey, and other edible goods that the disbelievers sell in the markets of the Muslims — is it permissible to eat such things or not? Answer: By Allah, success comes from Him. The sacrificial animals of the disbelievers are forbidden because they do not mention the name of Allah over them and do not slaughter them facing the qibla. As for their other foodstuffs and wet goods — if they have not handled them with their hands or their impure implements, there is no objection to selling, buying, or eating them, or having them serve a man. Their work is rarely free of impurity, however, because they are unclean and their implements touch wet goods. It is therefore obligatory to avoid what they have handled when an alternative exists. What is definitively forbidden is only their slaughtered animals, and their moisture is impure. If it falls onto a garment from the practices of the people of shirk, or if wet food has been

<!-- page 66 -->
and it is known that they defiled it, then whatever is on the garment or the body of the person must be washed.

Others have said: if a Jew, a Christian, a Magian, or a cat touches a large vessel, purification with it remains permissible unless saliva is evident. It is stated in questions from Sheikh Hasan ibn Nuh to our master Husayn ibn Idris — may Allah sanctify their souls — and in his answers to him: as for the slaughtered animals of the People of the Book, if a Muslim witnesses that they faced the qibla with it and invoked Allah's name over it, then they and the rest of their food are permissible, as stated in the text of the Holy Book. However, if they touch it with their moisture, it becomes impure, and Allah knows best.

It is stated in the brief exposition of the Shari'ah, in questions posed to the late Sheikh Hassan al-Hindi and his correspondence with the masters of Yemen — may Allah sanctify all their souls — that eating the cooking of a slave girl who is not careful about ritual purity is permissible.

**On the Matters of Natural Cleanliness and Purity**

In the first-mentioned book of questions and answers, arranged according to the order of the chapters of the book on the Pillars of Islam: Question: What do you say — O one who plunges into the current of knowledge — regarding the ruling that the lowest obligatory act in Islam may not be abandoned, namely circumcision, even if a man reaches eighty years of age? If a woman grows old and has not been reduced, should it be left or not? Answer: It should be reduced and not left unreduced. Question: What do you say, our master, our support, and our pillar, regarding the custom prevalent in India that

<!-- page 67 -->
men and women part their hair from the front of the head to the middle, and not down to the nape of the neck — does this distinguish them from one another or not? Answer: One should part the hair of the front of the head down to the nape of the neck, and should not deliberately leave it unparted at the back. Question: What do you say — O best of those before whose door the gates of good have opened for us — regarding the beard: if some hairs are long and some short, and the beard is trimmed so that the shorter hairs are leveled with the longer ones, intending thereby to improve its appearance — is there anything objectionable in that? Answer: It is permissible, because it is permissible to trim some of the offerings in such a way; otherwise, trimming them is not permissible. Question: What do you say — O one whom Allah has made a legislator of dignity for those who obey Allah — regarding the hair of the human body other than what there is a ruling to remove: must it be shaved, shortened, or plucked? Answer: One need not do anything with it except the beard — nothing may be removed from it. Question: What do you say — O one whom Allah has made a physician healing the souls of His guardians from all diseases — regarding a man and his wife sharing one comb to comb the head, something which some Indians dislike? Answer: There is no reliable basis for that dislike. Question: What do you say — O best of those who go forth with the light of his knowledge illuminating the insights of our eyes — regarding what is mentioned in the book of purity: that the first to be circumcised was Ibrahim, peace be upon him, at the age of eighty? Does this apparently indicate that this ruling and what follows it had not been revealed before that point, or not? Answer: This is apparently the case, because Ibrahim, peace be upon him, was not

<!-- page 68 -->
commanded to purify himself until he reached eighty years of age. Question: What do you say regarding what is mentioned in the book of purity — that the prayers of Allah be upon him — that he said: "O women who have circumcised, if you reduce your daughters, do not go to excess, and leave something of it, for it is better for the face and more pleasing to the husband" — O our master, is there a sin upon them if they go to excess? And what is the meaning of "it is better for the face"? Answer: One should act upon what has been mentioned; moderation is better for the husband's sake, and that is taken for granted. Question: What do you say — O best of those whom Allah has made a repository of the secrets of His guardians — regarding the words of the Prophet, peace be upon him and his family: "It is not for a woman who has menstruated to take a qaṣṣa or a qaṣṣa" — O our master, what does this mean? Answer: The nail of a thing refers to the portion from the temples to the nape of the neck. Al-qaṣṣa refers to cutting the hair of the head and leaving a quarter of it at the front. Al-qaṣṣa refers to taking the hair and leaving some of it out — and this too is forbidden. Question: What do you say — O one who draws forth knowledge — that most of the women of India engrave their palms and feet with henna for adornment, and some dye their teeth with a red substance or other materials also for adornment, while it is mentioned in the Shari'ah that engraving with dye is forbidden — what is their condition? Answer: Engraving with pigments and dyeing the teeth are forbidden. Question: What does our master say regarding a man who tattoos his hands and feet for the sake of adornment and not for any benefit — is that permissible or not? Answer: It is not permissible for a man to do so for adornment; women are commanded to adorn themselves, and leaving it is better for men

<!-- page 68 -->
without a reason, though the one who does it is not to be condemned outright. Question: What do you say — O treasure of wise gems — regarding a man or a woman whose teeth are uneven, some longer and some shorter, and who files them level with a stone, iron, or any other material: is there any sin in that? Please answer with a cure — may Allah redeem you. Answer: There is nothing wrong with that for women. Question: What do you say — O Bahr al-Mina — that the women of India pierce their own ears and the ears of their infant daughters, and look after those piercings so they can wear ornaments in them — is there anything objectionable in that for them or not? Answer: There is nothing wrong with that. Question: What do you say — O you who have the life of our souls — regarding a man who takes the hair of his head and, with a comb or without one, does not part it every day: is there any deficiency in his prayer, and how often should he comb and part his hair? Answer: If it is parted in two halves there is no deficiency, and it should not be left without a parting; if one deliberately leaves it unparted for the purpose of ruling, the prayer is invalidated. Question: What do you say — O one to whom the confused turn — regarding combing on Friday night? Most people in India are of the view that it is necessary to leave it out. Answer: There is no reliable basis for that. Question: What does our master say — may Allah bless him and grant him peace — regarding the ruling in the Shari'ah that the most important act is not left in Islam until a person is circumcised: should he be circumcised even after

<!-- page 69 -->
his death, whether he was a child or not? Answer: The Sunnah recommends that he be circumcised after death. Others have said: the prayer of a woman who has not been reduced, and her position with her husband, are valid — as is the case with a man.


<!-- page 70 -->
From the response of our master Judge al-Nu'man, may God sanctify his soul, to one who questioned him — and he was the ruler of his districts, al-Khattab ibn Wasim — regarding a man who was found to be ignorant: he had married a woman and fathered children with her, yet had not been circumcised. The question was whether he should be circumcised or not. The most ignorant person in Islam is not left uncircumcised, even if he reaches eighty years of age, unless he was born already circumcised. It has been narrated that some of the Prophets, peace be upon them, were born in that state. If the glans is exposed, he is not compelled to be circumcised, but it is recommended, if he is young, to roll back the foreskin and cut away whatever skin remains over the glans.

Our master and Mawlana Ja'far ibn Sulayman, may God sanctify him and grant us his intercession — in his noble letter, marked by the sharpest clarity in refuting the innovations about which he warned his people and admonished those who had grown heedless of them, from among the people of the Island of Yemen while he was among them — wrote as follows:

**Chapter:** We have come to learn that some of your womenfolk, in particular certain ones among your communities, are shaving their heads as men shave theirs. This practice, together with what we mentioned earlier, belongs to the category of what is forbidden — for the face and head are adorned differently according to gender. When any one of you reflects on this, the command will become clear: God Almighty has made the adornment of men in the growth of their beards, their grooming, and their care, along with their masculinity; and the adornment of women in the growth of their hair, its combing, its lengthening, and its beauty.

<!-- page 71 -->
It is established in the hadiths of the Prophet, peace be upon him and his family, that the shaved head of a woman is a source of shame for her, and that covering her head is obligatory except in cases of illness. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him and his family, said words to the effect that this removes the reverence of God from hearts. He also said: "If one of you wishes to marry a woman, let him inquire about her hair just as he inquires about her face, for hair is one of the two beauties." And it is narrated from him, peace be upon him and his family, that he said: "Beautiful hair is among the garments of God, and whoever strips his body of this garment of his Lord Almighty — with which He has clothed him — has disfigured his own form. Whoever disfigures his form is cursed in this world and the next."

It is astonishing that some have permitted this among themselves and within their law, while others have not been forbidden from it within theirs — yet all of them together have learned what is mentioned in the book *Pillars of Islam* concerning the hair of women's heads, which indicates the prohibition of shaving it, for the people of the Rightly Guided Da'wah and those who dwell within its reach. The author of that book, may God sanctify his soul, states: the imams of the Household of Prophethood, peace be upon them, said regarding a woman when she purifies herself: she should loosen her hair, except if she is certain that water reaches the skin of her head and wets all of her hair — and that is when her hair is soft and fine. He also said, in discussing the trimming of hair and the shortening required at the end of ihram for both men and women, and the distinction between them in the application of the laws of the purified Shari'ah — he grounded this discussion in established principles. He narrated from 'Ali, prayers of God be upon him, that he said regarding al-Aqra': the Musa passes over his head. And he, peace be upon him, said: if it is dissolved —

<!-- page 72 -->
— meaning when the woman exits her ihram, she takes from the tips of her braids. He also said, may God sanctify his soul and be pleased with him: we have mentioned that the bald man and the one with close-cropped hair have the Musa passed over their heads, while the woman, when she comes out of ihram, shortens her hair by cutting something from the ends — she does not shave any of it.

This ruling from him, may God sanctify his soul, is among the most conclusive proofs of the prohibition of women shaving their heads, because if even the small amount normally cut at the end of ihram is restricted — with many of them being prevented from that substitution — then how much more strictly is full shaving prohibited? The one who is certain that there is no such garment, whether young or old, is not included in this prohibition if he follows this path in this regard, for he has followed the way of guidance and removed the mark. Whoever desires a religion other than that of Ibrahim has only wronged himself. Then he said in a parable — may He, the Most Holy, provide us with His intercession and forgiveness.

**Chapter:** Among the things we have observed is that all women among you who remove their hair without shaving must shave it only to the tips of their heads — specifically at the circles of the head — and the shaving of the nape as well. This is something with which God Almighty is not pleased from those who do it among them; indeed, such a person is among those who are heading toward perdition, because it has already been narrated from the saying of our master Qadi al-Nu'man ibn Muhammad — may God provide us with his intercession and be pleased with him — we have mentioned that the bald and the close-cropped have the Musa passed over their heads, and the woman, when she breaks ihram, cuts something from the ends of her hair and does not shave any of it. For if it is —

<!-- page 73 -->
— not permissible for women to shave the ends of their hair even at the height of their ihram, then their heads must never be shaved at all; they may only cut from them what they cut — and that is never permissible to continue beyond, for this command never calls except to guidance. Whoever violates that among them has violated the obligations of the true religion and destroyed its foundation. Whoever desires a religion other than that of Ibrahim has only wronged himself. We set forth such a clarification only for the people of religion.

God Almighty says in this regard what has been brought by the Prophet, peace be upon him and his family — may God sanctify His Spirit, the Righteous, the Almighty.

**Chapter:** Among the things we have also observed is the hastening of the circumcision of your maidservants — your girls — in days fewer than ten, or above that up to forty days at most. This is the maximum period for your private and public practice, and it is contrary to the Shari'ah of the Prophet, peace be upon him and his family, and to the two pillars of Islam. What our master Judge al-Nu'man ibn Muhammad, may God sanctify his soul, mentioned in the book *Pillars of Islam al-Sharif* — narrating from your master the Commander of the Believers, 'Ali ibn Abi Talib, peace be upon him — where he stated clearly in explanation, and from him, peace be upon him, that he said: "Hasten the circumcision of your children, for it purifies them." And he said: "Do not circumcise the maidservant before she is seven years old." This is a clear prohibition that calls only to guidance.

It is never permissible to transgress it, for it is within the limits of God Almighty — the Arbitrator, blessed

<!-- page 74 -->
are His names. Whoever transgresses the limits has wronged himself. I have also observed that some of you, on the occasion of circumcising the maidservant, gather at that time those from among the family and neighbors who would gather for such an event, offer them hospitality, and have the call to prayer made and prayers established for those who remain. People carry news of the circumcision to those within earshot of the place, whether near or far, to both parents — and this is not part of the matter at hand.

Purity for male children and circumcision for maidservants is a private affair, to be witnessed only by the one being circumcised, the one performing the circumcision, and the women of the household. Whoever publicizes this among them has violated the laws of his religion and compounded that violation, acting contrary to the modesty of which the Prophet, peace be upon him and his family, said: "Modesty is from faith, and whoever has no modesty has no religion." On this basis, the circumcision of the maidservant carries one of the intended meanings in the matters of his dear religion.

It is not permissible to act against His will or transgress His command in what He has decreed, Almighty, in any of the matters that are bounded. This applies equally in all matters — their branches and their roots, every tangible matter whose rational counterpart is knowable, and every parable that is manifest with its inner meaning. Whoever departs from the path in this has invalidated it and veered from the way. It is not permissible to do that at all, as has been indicated by the guardians of God, peace be upon them, the guardians of their times, who warn. And God Almighty says in this regard: "Whatever the Messenger has brought you, take it; and what he has forbidden you, refrain from it." And then —

<!-- page 75 -->
Our master Ja'far, the Most High, may God sanctify him and reward us with his blessings and provisions — regarding the purity of children.

**Chapter:** Among the things we have also come to learn is that most of you delay the circumcision of your male children, who are commanded in the hadith of the Prophet to have it hastened — the hadith in which one of the Imams, peace be upon them, said: "Hasten the circumcision of your children, for it is purer for them." This is the opposite of how you treat the maidservants, so you have in fact reversed the matter in both cases, contrary to the command of the Dominant and the Righteous. It has been learned that people other than your own families among these communities raise their children to nearly twenty years of age before circumcising them — and this is a clear violation of the Shari'ah of Islam.

The wonder in this is that whoever administers these affairs ought to reflect on what this implies and understand that in this are signs for a people who think. For everyone with you who belongs to the people of the protected communities — the dhimmis — rushes to circumcise their children very quickly, doing so on the seventh day in compliance with what was prescribed for them in the law given by God Almighty to Moses, the Word of God, peace be upon him. They have continued to do this across thousands of years in tandem, persisting through the hardship it imposes on their children at such a young age. And you are closer than they are

— during the era of your Prophet, peace be upon him — and you are subject to his command in this matter. He commands you to hasten it, while you are engaged in the aforementioned deliberation that is contrary to the laws of Islamic law. Glory be to the great God —

<!-- page 76 -->
— glory be to God the great! How tremendous is your affair in this. God admonishes you that you never return to the like of it, if you are believers. God has made clear His verses to you, and God is All-Knowing, All-Wise.

Our master Ja'far, may God be pleased with him, said this within the context of the preceding matter in his honored letter, illuminating it with what he had set forth. The picture is now made clear.

**Chapter:** Among the things that have been learned and narrated from some of the callers among you in the mountain regions of the state — from them to the saints of God, peace be upon them — is that men imitate women from their households in dyeing their hands with henna, and some of them dye their hands and feet as well. This is not permissible by the law of the Prophet, peace be upon him and his family, nor by the religion of Islam, which has made this practice for women and not for men — it is among the adornments belonging exclusively to women and in no case to men. The man who does this and adopts it as an adornment imitates them, like one who adorns himself with ornaments of silver or gold that belong only to women and chooses this for himself — no one of sound mind would do so. Beware of this practice, for it is among the acts of disobedience to God Almighty. Replace it with fear of God Almighty, for you only reach what you hope for in all your affairs through the fear of God Almighty.

You have heard in the hadith that circulates in the Sunnah and is well known among the people and the Arabs: the adornment of men is conduct and character, while the adornment of women is gold. The opposites of the Guiding Da'wah and those who belong to it have already done what they were obligated to do, in accordance with the Lord of their affairs, the One who owns and will reckon —

<!-- page 77 -->
— and He will not let them persist in that detestable deed. God will grant from that a whitening of faces among you on the Day when faces whiten and faces turn black.

Our master Ja'far, may God sanctify him and grant us his intercession and forgiveness, said in answering questions that were posed to him by Maluka, the author of this book and his slave, who sent them to him from an island to his right — the Island of Hind. I asked about what is mentioned in the books of the purified Shari'ah: that maidservants are not to be circumcised before the age of seven years. So if one who has already passed below that limit is circumcised as male children are circumcised when they have not yet been circumcised in their lives — or is she to be buried as she is without circumcision? The intent is that if there is a prescribed age for her circumcision, that limit may not be transgressed.

Our master Muhammad ibn Fahd al-Mukarrami was asked about a girl who, before reaching seven years of age, dies — should she be circumcised or not? He replied: no, according to the questioner. She is not circumcised because she has not reached the age of circumcision.

And Mawlana Amir al-Mu'minin 'Ali ibn Abi Talib — for him God's peace be upon him — was asked, in his four hundred rulings: circumcise your children on the seventh day, and let no cold prevent you, for it is a purification of the body, and the earth is filled — by God — from the urine of the uncircumcised upon it.

**Mentioning the purity of skin, bones, hair, and wool**

He said in the first mentioned question-and-answer book, which is arranged in accordance with the arrangement of the chapters of the book *Pillars of Islam*: **Question:** What do you say, O Shu'a' —


<!-- page 78 -->
When the rays of guidance come into contact with impure garments, the skins of the dead, or the seven — if the body is wet — should the body be washed or not? The same question applies to clothes washed by the hands of polytheists. Answer: If the body comes into contact with such things, it must be washed, as must whatever the polytheists have washed with their hands. Question: What do you say regarding the interpretation of the pillars — that it is not permissible to shear a goat's hair as one shears a sheep's wool for benefit while the animal is alive, yet it is permissible to dress oneself in it, pray in it and upon it, and that the remainder of its hair, skins, and bones are free of impurity if the animal was slaughtered properly?

Should one pray on its hair and skins if they were taken after the animal was properly slaughtered? Answer: If the hair and skin are taken after proper slaughter, it is permissible to pray upon them. The goat is a variety of sheep, its defect beginning from its sin upward, and it has two horns — this is the most common type in India. Question: What of an animal that has been lawfully slaughtered — if some of its wool, fleece, or hair is cut while it is still alive, must it be washed like an impure garment, or not? And before it is washed, if it touches a garment or the body, does it render them impure or not? Answer: The text states that it is not permissible to pray in it until it is washed, though it does not itself make other things impure. Question: What does our master — may God perpetuate his reward — say regarding a vessel made from the skins of water animals, from one region of Vaghar (a large province), where most of the inhabitants are non-believers, and the vessel is filled with ghee or oil and brought to Ahmedabad for sale — is this permissible or not? And

<!-- page 79 -->
if this vessel is used to make soap by mixing lye and soda ash, is it permissible to sell and buy such soap? Answer: If this vessel is made of something that renders its contents impure, then the ghee becomes impure, and it is not permissible to pray in a garment that has been soiled by it. It is permissible to sell and buy it, provided the buyer knows that the ghee or soap is impure. It is not permissible, however, to sell or buy the skins of the dead. The Book of the Interpretation of the Pillars states that the goat's hair may not be cut except after slaughter — while it is alive, this ruling applies in full — and it further states that it is not permissible to shear a goat's hair as one shears a sheep's wool for benefit while it is still alive, though it is permissible to make clothing from it and to pray in it and upon it. The remainder of its hair, skin, and bones are free of impurity. They are purified if the animal is properly slaughtered. From the summary of works: if a person has bones, it is not permissible to patch them except with the bone of an animal whose flesh may lawfully be eaten after proper slaughter. If one patches with a bone from a dead animal, or from an animal whose flesh is not eaten, or with the bone of a human being, then that dead person has a ruling that applies to him: he must repeat every prayer he has prayed, and the authority compels him — if he was aware of this — to remove it. If he was unaware until he died and did not remove it, no removal takes place after his death. One may not benefit from the hide of a human being as one benefits from the hide of an animal. From the Book of the Interpretation of the Pillars, regarding the benefit of a quilt made from the dead that does not adhere — meaning it does not stick to anything pure — yet one of them is wet, making it a source of impurity. And from the Book of Reports: they considered it reprehensible to make use of pork.

<!-- page 80 -->
**Menstruation and Postpartum**

It is said in the aforementioned book of questions and answers, arranged according to the chapters of the book *The Pillars of Islam*: Question: What do you say has been established in Islamic law — that a menstruating woman is to wear a garment from the navel to the knees, and her husband may approach her above the garment? Without such a garment, O our master, may he begin with her entire body without intercourse in the vulva? Answer: Yes, he may begin with her body, though the first obligation is that he avoid what is beneath the garment, out of concern that fluid may reach her womb. Additionally: if a woman menstruates and the bleeding continues for the full ten days, and then after those ten days it continues for another two, three, four, or five days, she does not pray, does not fast, and her husband does not approach her. She does not need to make up those prayers after purification if it is confirmed that the blood is indeed menstrual blood. If there is doubt about the blood, then she is considered a mustahadah, and so forth.

Whoever misses the Eid al-Ghadir prayer and its fast may make it up on any day after that, as if it were performed at its proper time. Question: What do you say regarding a woman who becomes pure from menstruation or postpartum bleeding at the time of prayer — if she finishes her ritual bath just as the prayer time ends, must she make up that prayer or not? Answer: If she completed the bath in good time — that is, her washing was sound — yet the time passed before she could pray, she is not required to make up that prayer. And from the answers

<!-- page 81 -->
of our master al-Nu'man — may God sanctify his soul — to the ruler of Zawatah, Khattab bin Waseem: he was asked about a woman who is blind and inquired about her menstruation and how she should determine it. She should take a white piece of cotton or cloth and have it checked by a woman she trusts in her religion. If the cloth comes out with color, she is menstruating. If it comes out white, she has become pure, and she should wash, pray, fast, and allow her husband to come to her. She follows the same procedure at the beginning of her menstrual cycle if she cannot sense it herself. Additionally: if a woman becomes pure from menstrual blood during the month of Ramadan before sunset but fears that performing ghusl would harm her, she may perform tayammum and fast, and she does not need to make up that fast. The same applies to a man who experiences a nocturnal emission during Ramadan — if he fears illness from washing that night, he may fast without ghusl, and his fast is valid. Question: What of a woman who becomes pure in the month of Ramadan from menstruation and fasts for nine days, then menstruation returns to her on the tenth day — is her fast for those nine days valid? Answer: It has been mentioned that the minimum of menstruation is three days and the minimum of purity is ten days. Question: From the Book of Purity — if the days of menstruation exceed ten days and the bleeding extends beyond this period, is her fast during the separation period valid, even if the blood is thick and strong? Answer: The distinction between menstrual blood and istihadah blood has been mentioned along with the differences between them, so rely on the relevant text in this matter. Question: In Islamic law, the maximum limit of postpartum bleeding is forty days, while some books state sixty days — which is more correct, O our master? Answer: This answer comes from

<!-- page 82 -->
our master Hassan bin Noah — may God sanctify his soul. He said: our master — may God perpetuate his soul — reckons the postpartum period according to what the woman is accustomed to in duration, and the blood of the puerperium is distinguished from the blood of istihadah. When she becomes pure and the blood stops, she performs ghusl, arrives at prayer, and remains silent. Her husband may then approach her. It is stated in the Book of Purity and Souls: if a woman gave birth, or saw blood before giving birth in a state of menstruation, and the blood continued until it ceased — then the menstruating woman washes when she becomes pure. If her bleeding is excessive, she is a mustahadah. The blood of the puerperium is distinguished from the blood of istihadah just as we have mentioned the distinction of menstrual blood from istihadah blood. He said in the summary of effects and souls: she is like a menstruating woman, and she and her husband must observe what is obligatory in her affairs during menstruation. She should wash when the blood of the puerperium is cut off from her. He said in the summary of the Musannaf: the soul — meaning the woman in the postpartum period — leaves prayer for the number of days she is accustomed to, as has been her habit, as well as in the *Pillars of Islam*, in which this course is laid out. And I saw in a book of Shariah works for the Ahl al-Bayt — prayers of Allah upon them — several mentions of the soul in terms of days. It is mentioned therein that the minimum postpartum period is twenty days, the middle is forty days, and the maximum is sixty days. If the blood continues beyond this, it shall not exceed sixty days. Menstrual blood is distinct from istihadah blood, and the postpartum period does not fall below twenty days. The puerperium is never considered pure before twenty days — this is what is meant by the words in that book. As for the exact phrase from the original source, I did not find it at the time of writing this paper,

<!-- page 83 -->
so I have not written the exact phrase. But if I live to receive it and find that book, I will write its exact phrase, God willing.

What is meant is that I have shown you that when the woman in the postpartum period is free from blood, she is considered pure after twenty days — this ruling applies to her definitively. She then follows her accustomed pattern thereafter. If her bleeding exceeds her habit, she observes whether the blood has changed in character. If it has changed, then it is istihadah blood. If it has not changed, then its ruling is as I first described. Our master al-Nu'man — may Allah sanctify his soul — has been quoted in *The Chosen One* as saying:

The woman in her postpartum period sits for as long as her menstruation

If her blood goes on too long

I have been waiting for the equivalent of two months

Then she continued as things were during those two times

A wall was cleansed, greeting

From prayer and fasting after

If she menstruated after this expiration

I have mentioned its ruling previously.

This applies when the blood of the puerperium extends beyond its customary duration and the blood remains thick, like the blood of the puerperium. In such a case, she ceases prayer and fasting and abstains from her husband for two months. After two months, if the blood continues beyond that, she is undoubtedly a mustahadah. She washes and makes up the prayers and fasts that were omitted after the end of her habitual period up to exactly two months, as a precaution, because this is a matter of doubt — she may have menstruated following the postpartum period. This is the ruling on postpartum bleeding, and Allah and His Messenger know best. Our master's answer has been completed — praise be to Allah and from Him. He said in the book *Mukhtasar al-Musannaf*: if she

<!-- page 84 -->
prays at the proper time and then menstruates, she does not need to make up that prayer. If she menstruates for one or two days, then the bleeding stops and she sees purity for ten days, there is nothing wrong with this — it is not problematic — unless the menstrual period is less than three days. If she does not pray during that time, she must repeat her prayers. If she sees blood for a day or two, then sees purity for less than ten days, then sees blood again — the first and second instances of blood are counted as menstruation. This is mentioned in the Book of Ta'wil al-Da'a'im under the topic of nifas; rather, it is mentioned at the time of birth. What is meant by the blood that occurs at that time is illustrated by what was narrated from the Messenger of Allah — may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him — when he said to one of his wives who was with him in his bed, confirming what she herself testified: "Yes" — meaning she had menstruated. The mustahadah is the one from whom blood does not stop, but it is distinct from menstrual blood. When her menstruation comes, the menstrual blood comes thick. When her menstrual period passes, the blood becomes thin. What is therefore obligatory for her: as long as the menstrual blood is present, she is in a state of menstruation — she does not pray, does not enter the mosque, and her husband does not approach her. When the menstrual blood ceases and is replaced by the other blood, she purifies herself as she would from menstruation, then she prays, fasts in a state of purity, enters the mosque, and allows her husband to approach her. Whatever blood issues from that state requires ablution, and this is the ruling on it. From the Book of the Interpretation of the Pillars: whoever approaches a menstruating woman and does what he should not have done must seek God's forgiveness and repent to Him for his sin, and give charity despite the fact that he

<!-- page 85 -->
has sinned. The blood of the puerperium is distinguished from the blood of istihadah, just as we have mentioned the distinction of menstrual blood from istihadah blood. From the Book of the Interpretation of the Pillars as well: the wife must obey her husband if he calls her, and if he dislikes her, nothing is upon her. If the man does not know about her condition — even if it is her own secret — then the sin lies with her, and nothing is upon her if he does not know that she is in her menstrual period. A menstruating woman should not touch the Quran. He said in the questions that came from Shaykh Shimon ibn Muhammad Ghori al-Hindi to our master Hassan bin Idris — may God bless him. Question: What do you say, O pillar of the Da'wah, regarding the postpartum period — the general public claims that the minimum and maximum limit is forty days. Answer: It may be less than that, though forty days is not a requirement. And I asked, O Lord, the Glorious and Wise Father, about menstruation that exceeds ten days: if it exceeds ten days, she is a mustahadah. Our master and our dear father Muhammad bin al-Fahd — may God exalt his rank — says that if a woman sees blood ten days after her menstruation, she should wash each time she is able, and pray. If she cannot perform ghusl, she performs tayammum and prays. If she can wash even at a time other than the prayer time, she should do so. If she cannot, she performs tayammum at the time of prayer when she cannot wash and then prays. He said in the book of questions from the late Shaykh Simeon ibn Muhammad Ghori al-Hindi — may Allah be pleased with him — and the answer from our master Badr


<!-- page 86 -->
On the question of the world and religion: al-Hasan ibn Idris — may God sanctify him and grant us his intercession — posed the following question: What do you say regarding one who is in a state of major ritual impurity (janābah) or menstruation and wishes to recite supplications or other texts that contain Quranic verses — whether the reciter is aware of this or not — should such a person recite them or refrain? It is established in the Sharīʿah that a person in a state of janābah or menstruation may not recite the Quran or touch it. Furthermore, should a person in such a state recite the tahlīl, takbīr, tahmīd, and tasbīh, given that all of these appear in the Quran? And should such a person recite the basmala, given the position that it is itself a verse of the Quran?

Answer: A woman in a state of menstruation, or a person in a state of janābah, may not recite the Quran — nor any portion of it — whether in supplication or in written form. Nor may such a person recite the basmala with the intention of Quranic recitation. Hold firmly to what I have mentioned to you, brother, and be among the believers who worship their Lord until certainty comes to them.

**On the Mention of Disavowal**

It is stated in the aforementioned book of questions and answers — arranged in the order of the chapters of the Book of the Pillars of Islam — that whoever commits adultery with a virgin woman then wishes to marry her, the marriage contract is not permissible until after she has been discharged. If a man has a wife who has a son from another man, and that child dies leaving an estate behind, then her husband must observe a waiting period from her until her womb is cleared. If she is pregnant, the child inherits from the deceased, but only if the mother is neither a slave nor a polytheist — for in such cases there is no valid inheritance claim. This is the ruling even if

<!-- page 87 -->
the mother is a free Muslim woman, in which case the inheritance belongs to her and not to the brothers. She need not observe a waiting period in such a case. It is stated in the summary of legal opinions that a free woman observes a waiting period of two months. It is stated in the book of transmitted reports that scholars held that a woman who has reached forty-five nights is considered to have completed her waiting period, and that for one who is young and reaches the age of menstruation, the seller must declare that she has been discharged for nine months. It is stated in the Book of Adornments that the child belongs to the bed, and that the fornicator is entitled to nothing — he has no claim to the child's lineage. It is stated in the aforementioned book of questions and answers that if a man commits adultery with a woman and then purchases her while she is pregnant, and does not approach her until she gives birth, the child may be sold — for the child belongs to the bed and the fornicator has no claim.

**The Book of Prayer**

**On the Virtues of Prayer, What Is Forbidden in It, and Related Matters**

The Commander of the Faithful, ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib — peace be upon him — said in his four hundred precepts of good conduct: Keeping vigil at night is beneficial for the body, pleasing to the Lord, a means of being exposed to divine mercy, and a way of following the conduct of the Prophets. He said: A man should not stand to pray in a state of laziness, nor should he pray while

<!-- page 88 -->
drowsy. The servant should reflect, when standing before God Almighty, that someone may accept his prayer while another does not. One should not pray while distracted by remembrance of worldly things, and none of you should pay attention to anything outside his prayer — for if the servant turns away, God Almighty says:

I am better for you than that toward which you have turned. He said: Beware of laziness, for whoever is overcome by laziness will not fulfill his true obligations to God Almighty. He said: None of you should fidget with his beard during prayer or with anything that distracts him from it. He said: Prayer is an offering for every pious person. He said: There is no deed more beloved to God than prayer, so let not the affairs of this world distract you from it or cause you to be heedless of its times — for God Almighty reproached a people who were careless about its times, saying: ⟪ar:فَوَيْلٌ لِّلْمُصَلِّينَ الَّذِينَ هُمْ عَن صَلَاتِهِمْ سَاهُونَ⟫ — meaning: heedless.

It is stated in a book of legitimate legal questions — each one posing a difficulty that is incapable of resolution by another — the following: What do you say about a man for whom prayer is not permissible, yet who is not excused for omitting it? Answer: Such a man is one who has consumed alcohol until he lost his reason; prayer is therefore not permissible for him on account of his drinking, yet he is not excused for abandoning it, in light of the words of God Almighty: ⟪ar:لَا تَقْرَبُوا الصَّلَاةَ وَأَنتُمْ سُكَارَىٰ⟫. Consider what you say. He said: Another question — what do you say about a man who prayed in a state of ritual purity, yet his prayer is not permissible? Answer: Such is the case of a muhājir who prayed behind Arabs whose interpretations are not accepted, because the Arabs in question are disbelievers and hypocrites, and it is not permissible to pray behind them. He said: Another question — what do you say about an obligatory prayer that requires neither bowing nor prostration? Answer: Such is the case of a person floating in water who is unable to reach dry land; such a person need not bow or prostrate.

<!-- page 89 -->
**On the Mention of Prayer Times and Related Matters**

The following is from the aforementioned book of questions and answers, arranged according to the order of the chapters of the Book of the Pillars of Islam.

Question: What do you say — O you whose authority is acknowledged by the greatest of scholars — regarding what is stated in the Book of Purity: that if a man remembers missed prayers (al-fāʾitāt) at the time of a current prayer, and there is sufficient time in that prayer window, should he pray the missed prayers first and then pray the current prayer in its time? Or should he leave the missed prayers and perform the current prayer on time?

Answer: Yes — he is sinning if he delays while time remains and nothing prevents him from praying.

Question: What do you say regarding what is stated in the Book of Pillars — that when the Messenger of God, may God bless him and his family, prayed the Fajr prayer after its time had passed, he began with the Sunnah before the obligatory prayer. How then should one who has missed the Sunnah of Fajr proceed and make it up at Dhuhr?

Answer: The Sunnah of Fajr is offered as a make-up (qaḍāʾ) after the missed prayers (al-fāʾitāt).

Question: What does our master — may God perpetuate his support — say regarding a man who prayed two or four rakʿahs of the Sunnah and then prayed the obligatory prayer in congregation — should he perform the remaining Sunnah or make it up?

Answer: What remains of the Sunnah is to be made up, provided the time for the Sunnah has not elapsed — but not for the Maghrib. Question: What does

<!-- page 90 -->
our master — may God perpetuate his support — say regarding a man who prayed the obligatory prayer with the imam and missed the Sunnah: should he perform the Sunnah before the optional prayers (nawāfil), or make it up?

Answer: There is nothing wrong with his praying the optional prayers, as long as time has not elapsed — and the preferable course is to perform the optional prayers first and then make up the Sunnah.

Question: What does our master — may God perpetuate his support — say regarding a man who prays the obligatory prayer at the very end of its time and misses the time — should he then pray optional prayers or make up what he missed?

Answer: He should pray optional prayers, since he has already performed the obligatory prayer.

Question: What does our master — may God perpetuate his support — say regarding a man who prays the obligatory Maghrib prayer and then speaks while the people are praying the Sunnah, then goes out to visit a relative and returns to find the people still praying the Sunnah — should he pray the Sunnah in congregation or make it up?

Answer: If he prayed the Sunnah within the appropriate time and then departed, he has performed it even if it was late — in which case he should make it up. And if he went out briefly and returned while the people were still in the Sunnah prayer, he may join them and it counts as a performance.

Question: What does our master — may God perpetuate his support — say regarding a man who arrives and joins the people in the ʿAṣr prayer at noon time, knowing that he is praying ʿAṣr — should he intend Dhuhr or ʿAṣr with the imam, or should he pray both ʿAṣr and Dhuhr separately without the imam?

Answer: If he enters and follows the imam in the ʿAṣr prayer while it is still noon time, he may intend Dhuhr with him. But if the time for Dhuhr has elapsed, he prays ʿAṣr with the imam if he wishes, and then makes up Dhuhr — though praying behind the imam in this case is considered commendable.

Question: Regarding a worshipper who owes the obligatory Dhuhr prayer but sets it aside to perform optional prayers, working until the end of the Dhuhr time and then wishing to pray — is his prayer then a performance or

<!-- page 91 -->
a make-up?

Answer: Our master said — may God perpetuate his support — that if he is within the remaining time, his optional prayer is still a performance.

Question: Regarding a person inside the mosque at the time of an eclipse who prays the Dhuhr prayer six rakʿahs and then remembers that he still has a Sunnah on him that he missed earlier — should he make up those missed Sunnahs between the eclipse prayers and the obligatory Dhuhr prayers, or not?

Answer: If he prays the Sunnah of Dhuhr at the beginning of the prayer, there is nothing wrong with his making up the Sunnah between it and the obligatory prayer.

Question: If a prayer is performed within its proper time it is a performance, and if the time has elapsed it is a make-up — so how does a worshipper intend the Sunnah of Maghrib if he prays it only after some time has passed since Maghrib?

Answer: The Sunnah of Maghrib is to be prayed after the obligatory prayer, without any separation between them — even something minor. The congregation should not delay the Sunnah of Maghrib to perform additional voluntary prayers.

Question: Regarding a person who prays the Dhuhr prayer and then, upon finishing, recalls that he has missed Dhuhr prayers to make up — should he make up what he prayed by way of qaḍāʾ and resume the performance, or not?

Answer: This is not the right approach. Rather, he should simply make up what he missed. As for voluntary prayers between the obligatory prayer and the Hereafter: she need not make them up; if he wishes to pray optional prayers, he does not intend them as make-up, for they are called the night prayer.
