Since sight only perceives shapes and colors, and the request regarding the crescent was not to know its color and shape, if it had been requested to know its color and shape, that would already be impossible to request from one who already has knowledge of how it looks in shape and color from having seen it with the eye and trusts that it will not change as long as the heavens and the earth endure, and that it will not differ from the image he perceived in the state of its rotation — in form, illumination, from its position to a certain configuration in shape and illumination, or to an entirely different configuration. Similarly, his seeking it to suspend the obligation of fasting on the condition of precision of light and coarseness —

<!-- page 267 -->
if he had been asked to know its precision and coarseness, it would have been impossible to make fasting obligatory if it is precise while the condition is coarseness, or fasting if it is coarse while the condition is precision. This condition of shape, color, precision, and coarseness was not present in the obligation of fasting, so it is invalid to say that the obligation of fasting is attached to the perception of the crescent by sight. Once this is invalidated, it is established that fasting is attached to the perception of the soul, because what is sought is whether the crescent is in front of the sun. The indication is that man knows through inference what he perceives from physical things through their perceptions — things which the senses do not directly perceive — and that what is meant by seeking the crescent is to know its being and its precision and coarseness, such that knowledge of it can only come through the soul. If fasting is thus attached to the soul's vision and not to anyone else's, then the people of the da'wah who enter fasting according to the soul's vision are on a straight and upright path. As for sight-vision, it may err in perceiving things as they really are — its symptoms being that it sees the large as small and the small as large, the fine as coarse and the coarse as fine; it may only see from near and not from far, or from far and not from near; and it can barely err in what it encompasses. Taking what does not err in establishing obligatory duties, according to the people of hearts, is more deserving than taking what does err. So when the people of the call entered the fast, they took what does not err — on a clear and established basis. Then the saying of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him): "Fast upon seeing it and break the fast upon seeing it,"

<!-- page 268 -->
is not without two possible interpretations. Either he intended by this saying that the fasting of the Ummah

<!-- page 268 -->
should begin at the time of seeing the crescent with the eyes — as the opponents say — and the crescent is seen by the eyes and the Ummah fasts as we have said; or the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) intended the fasting of the Ummah from the time of the crescent's sighting with the eye. In that case, what is the meaning of saying "upon seeing it," and what is the reason for his restricting the expression to this particular form, given his distinction and his saying "I am the most eloquent of the Arabs" — and there is no significance in the lam without some other purpose being served by the expression? Was this use of the lam by the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) in this place — saying "to see it" rather than otherwise — intended to mean that the Ummah should fast when seeing the crescent by sight? The meaning lies in the word used and what is intended is that the action precede its effect, as when it is said to a group: "Prepare such-and-such a thing for the coming of so-and-so" — meaning that the thing should be prepared at the time of his coming. And as it is said: "They composed a poem to meet so-and-so, to recite it to him" — meaning that they will have it ready when they find him, not that they compose it from the moment of meeting him. And as it is said: "Work for the Hereafter" — meaning they should do the work before the time of the Hereafter arrives, for it is known that in the Hereafter there is no work. With these evidences it is established that the lam was not used to mean that the people fast when seeing the crescent with their own eyes. Even if it were the intention of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that the crescent be seen and the Ummah fast upon it, that is what we have said, and we have demonstrated its proof. And if our opponent says: "You do not deny that the lam is from the words of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) — see this lam as from the words of Allah Almighty: 'Establish the prayer for'

<!-- page 269 -->
the sun" — it does not require that the prayer precede the sun's decline, nor that the prayer be offered before the sun. We say: the saying regarding the lam in the saying of Allah Almighty about the sun is like the saying regarding the lam in the saying of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) — "to see it" — because the sun's decline is its disappearance from the dome of the sky, as has been explained. The perception of this decline is of two kinds: psychological perception and natural perception. Psychological perception precedes natural perception, because the decline in reality within the dome of the heavens can only be perceived by the soul through the perspective of knowledge — since natural vision is limited in its perception until the sun reaches the azimuth point and descends, securing its range in its descent at the intersection of the circle line and the straight line, as shown in this picture.

Then the sense will perceive it, because the movement of the sun and its shadow are within the reach of the senses by virtue of its elevation in the arc when it rises from the east and descends toward the west. When it has no more rising and falling, the senses perceive the decrease and increase of the shadow. This occurs in the sun where it has no height and is moving toward its decline and descent. The senses grow weary of perceiving its course and movement because the sun has reached the azimuth point — so that its movement becomes hidden from the senses and absent from them. One who does not know might suppose that it has stopped, yet it is only hidden from the senses because it has entered the position from which it is at its farthest distance, having risen above it, and its movement has now passed into the azimuth of the circle line along a plane line that is tangent to it.


<!-- page 271 -->
The surface of the straight circle line extends from position A, where it intersects the circle and the plane line from east to west. The senses do not perceive the shadow decreasing or increasing except through the sun's rising and setting, and the sun has no rise or fall except along a flat line.

This being so, the true disappearance that makes prayer obligatory occurs when the sun's disk crosses line B — perceived by psychic vision — which marks the halfway point of its daily arc, not when it reaches line C, which is perceived by natural vision and corresponds to more than half of that arc. Reaching line B involves descent even when the senses cannot detect the shadow's movement or the sun's displacement, because the sun is moving along a flat line and its passage only becomes apparent to the senses after it crosses the intersection of the circle line and the plane line. The discussion of the sun's rays parallels what was said about seeing it: the shadow must then exist. The worshipper is praying in his prayer just as the crescent moon is inhabited and perceived by the senses, and the fasting person is in his fast, both being cautious in fulfilling their obligations. With these testimonies the opposing view is invalidated.

If someone objects and says: "If the matter is as you have described, and fasting is obligatory on the basis of psychic vision as you maintain, then what is the meaning of the Prophet's words (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him): 'If you are obscured, then count thirty days'?" — that is not an objection against psychic vision. We say: we have established psychic vision through the evidence we have presented, and it is more honourable

<!-- page 272 -->
and more appropriate to apply it in fulfilling obligatory duties. We therefore did not restrict the ruling to naked-eye vision alone, because both modes of vision are foundational in performing obligatory duties, and one is superior to the other. The superior mode belongs to the select, who are advanced in knowledge and are the first to understand. The other belongs to the general public, who are obliged to follow the people of knowledge and who lack the insight to distinguish between the two modes of vision. The relationship between the two is like that between two prayer times, the earlier of which is superior to the later.

This is true of all things: they have hidden and apparent dimensions. The hidden is known only by those of virtue, while the apparent is known by both the select and the general public alike. The select are obliged to act upon what they have learned, just as the common people are obliged to act upon what they have come to know. This being so, no one can deny that a scholar is better placed to know when fasting is obligatory through hidden psychic vision, and that the ignorant must follow the scholar in this — just as when an astronomer, through his hidden knowledge, knows that dawn has arrived and that eating, drinking, and sexual relations are forbidden and fasting is obligatory, the one who is ignorant of dawn's timing must follow his guidance.

The Prophet's statement (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) — "If you are obscured then count thirty days" — is scarcely correct if one takes it to mean visible naked-eye sighting, because fasting with sight depends on obstacles such as clouds clearing away. Such obstacles may mean that the month falls short of thirty days and stands at only twenty-nine. If that were the case, it would not be permissible for the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) to

<!-- page 273 -->
leave his community in one of Allah's commands subject to a rule that may generate doubt — which would cause either an increase or a decrease in an obligation, leaving his community to perform it haphazardly. Nor would it be right for him to command his nation to fast an extra day in a month that falls short, when but for that command it would have been known that fasting on that day is forbidden and breaking the fast is required, since fasting is an act of worship of Allah Almighty and one of the religious duties in which Allah commands His servants to be sincere. As Allah the Almighty says: "And they were not commanded except to worship Allah, sincere to Him in religion." Sincerity stands apart from doubt and suspicion, and doubt and suspicion are dismissed by this principle.

This establishes that the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) did not expose people to doubt and suspicion in their worship of Allah Almighty. Since the Prophet (peace be upon him) did not call for doubt and suspicion, the days of the month are fixed at their fullest and most complete count — without deficiency or reduction — and the permissibility of the month being incomplete according to naked-eye sighting, along with the doubt attached to it, was only intended to point toward the vision of the soul, whose count neither increases nor decreases. It was an exhortation to follow what Allah Almighty called for in completing the prescribed period, as He said: "And complete the 'iddah." We therefore say: Where does the obligation lie for those whose fast falls during overcast skies, making it forbidden

<!-- page 274 -->
for one person — whether he is in Ramadan or in Sha'ban, with clouds in the sky — while he counts that particular day, yet for other people of his own school, following naked-eye sighting and contending with their own obstacles such as clouds, the fast may differ? For example, in Kirman where overcast skies prevail, the fast differs from the fast of Shiraz where, say, there are no clouds — even though it may be permissible for the people of one region to fast at the time of fasting.

This variation is so great that fasting becomes obligatory for some people on one day and for other people on the following day. Similarly, prayer times may vary between regions to the point that a prayer becomes obligatory for one person at a given time while it is obligatory for another person at a different time. Since that cannot be permissible, a prayer time comes in which the whole community is obliged to establish it together, and whoever delays it and abandons it has neglected an obligation. In the same way, when the time comes for the community to fast, whoever fasts and whoever breaks the fast in it are both bound by that time.

This being so, what the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said was an indication that while error may fall in naked-eye sighting, error does not fall in the sighting of the soul — which is knowledge — and his promise that one of its outcomes is that the one entrusted with it completes the count. The Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) therefore said: "Count the days of Sha'ban," and breaking the fast is to be determined by counting the days of Ramadan. It is known that counting is only possible by means of a counter and by knowing the first day of the month. It is thus known that in most months the crescent moon cannot be sighted at the outset, especially in places where

<!-- page 275 -->
the air is turbid, such as Tabaristan, the mountain regions, the coasts, the sea islands, and so forth. If it were possible for the crescent moon not to be visible at the beginning of Sha'ban to the naked eye — owing to some impediment — then one would not know from that how to prepare for it or how to prepare for Ramadan, whose beginning had not yet been established. Carrying this report on the meaning of naked-eye sighting implies that the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) and his family led his nation into perplexity and called them to what they are unable to do.

Whoever counts the days of the month without knowing its beginning and without seeing the crescent cannot fulfill this duty. Allah Almighty says: "Allah does not burden a soul beyond what it can bear." Since attributing this to the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) and his family is inconceivable — for he is exalted above burdening the servants of Allah with what they cannot do, or casting them into dispute — then the report "If you are obscured, count thirty days" must either be incorrect, or the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) and his family said it in a sense that is sound and that settles the matter properly, without ambiguity, for him and for everyone else. If that is the case, the testimonies confirm that the one to whom creation is entrusted is the vision of the rational soul. We say: now that this discussion has established that what is intended is not naked-eye sighting and that the general public who possess knowledge must follow accordingly — and the opposing view on this has been invalidated — we further say that any pillar of Islam brought by

<!-- page 276 -->
Muhammad (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) and his family — whether prayer, fasting, zakat, Hajj, or jihad — is entrusted to the infallible community that is safeguarded from error. In prayer, for instance, all its acts — the opening takbir, recitation, bowing, prostration, and submission

<!-- page 276 -->
to the Imam standing in place of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) in leading the people — require the believers to follow the Imam in these acts without introducing or delaying any of them on their own initiative. Whoever introduces or delays any act in his prayer has deviated. Is it not the case that zakat is to be collected by the standing Imam in place of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him)? It is not permissible for him to distribute what he receives from those who respond to him without the community's involvement, as it was in the days of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him). Whoever gave his zakat

<!-- page 277 -->
to an authority other than the one holding the Prophet's office (peace be upon him) would have been required to repeat it to him by placing it properly, just as one who gave his zakat during the days of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) would have had to give it again to him in the appropriate manner. Is it not the case that Hajj is only performed under an Imam who leads the group, so they must follow him in the rituals? Whoever performs a ritual ahead of or behind the others disrupts the order, and an individual Hajj is not valid. Similarly, jihad for the sake of Allah requires whoever takes charge of the Muslims to preserve their order and all their affairs — advancing them in fighting, withdrawing, pursuing their interests in truces and in battle. The mujahid community must follow the command of Allah without following their own desires in peace and in war. Whoever fights without his command and sheds blood without his knowledge is on a leash.

This being so, and since the pillars of Islam are entrusted to those who preserve them,

<!-- page 277 -->
the community must follow its leader in them. How then is it permissible for people to enter the fast — which is one of those pillars — on the basis of their own individual judgement, without following the one who stands in place of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) in this matter? And did that interruption lead them to enter the fast through their own ijtihad, which causes them to treat what is not obligatory as obligatory and what is obligatory as abandoned, or to count as a Sunnah what is in fact a violation of Allah's command and a departure from the infallibility of His authority? We seek refuge in Allah from that.

If someone says: "According to your principle — which is that the beginning of fasting requires concurrence, and that the irony must fall on a single day — the concurrence implies that some of those days are from Sha'ban and some are from Ramadan, and it becomes obligatory for some to fast in a manner the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) did not command." We say: the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) and his family imposed the obligatory duties by Allah's command and established for each obligation

<!-- page 278 -->
a preceding cause that is general — in the sense that it is a cause for that particular obligation and for other obligations as well — and a second cause that is specific and particular to that obligation alone, distinguishing it from the others. For example, the first prayer Allah enjoined during the day — not at night. We say that this prayer is not obligatory except during the day, and the day is a preceding cause that is general in the sense that it is a cause for this prayer and for other prayers performed during the day. Yet this prayer is not obligatory with the mere occurrence of this preceding cause, which is the day, except when the sun has passed

<!-- page 278 -->
its zenith. We say that this is a second condition that must be taken into account, and it is specific — like the zakat that He imposed. We say that zakat was not supposed to be given except in the presence of wealth, and wealth is the general preceding cause for the obligation of zakat; yet zakat is not obligatory with the mere occurrence of that preceding cause until a full year has elapsed. The passage of a year is therefore a second condition that must be taken into account, and it is specific — like the Hajj that He imposed. We say that Hajj is not supposed to be performed except when it is possible for the person to do so, and ability is the general preceding cause; yet Hajj is not obligatory with the occurrence of that preceding cause except on the greatest day in Mecca. We say that this is a second cause to be taken into account in its ruling, and it is specific — like the jihad that Allah Almighty imposed on the community. We say that jihad is only supposed to be waged when there is discord and when the banner of disobedience and defiance of authority is raised, which is the general preceding cause; yet jihad is not obligatory in the presence of discord and defiance of authority except through the standing of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) and a leader who leads the community into battle. We say that this is a second cause and it is specific. Since that is the case, we say that fasting likewise has a general preceding cause, which is the conjunction of the moon and the sun, and a specific second cause, which is the time of dawn at which one must enter

<!-- page 278 -->
into fasting — just as prayer is not obligatory during the day until the day reaches its end in a particular respect.


<!-- page 279 -->
As for prayer — its time is due even if wealth is withheld, and zakat is due when wealth reaches its nisab at the appointed time, and Hajj is due with the possibility of performing the standing on the greatest day at its appointed time, and jihad is obligatory only when a divinely guided leader — one who stands in place of the Lord of all worlds — is present to lead the community. In the same way, fasting is not obligatory merely because the moon exists at dawn.

After establishing this, he refuted the objection of his opponent, who held that since a given period spans part of Sha'ban and part of Ramadan, only the Ramadan portion should be fasted — a view that in fact strengthens the position of others. The matter is thus settled by the evidence of the great outward testimony: the testimony that bears witness to the truthfulness of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, in what he conveyed from Allah Almighty — a balance between the revealed Law and its religious obligations on one side, and what Allah Almighty has created in His natural order among the horizons and within souls on the other, as stipulated in the words of Allah: *"We will show them Our signs on the horizons and within themselves until it becomes clear to them that it is the truth."* No human being could imitate the Prophet in his actions except one uniquely prepared in the barn of Jerusalem, set apart by his attribute and his readiness to receive the revelation of Allah Almighty as a gift and a preparation. This, if we return to interpretation, is one of the two oaths by which Allah commanded His Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, to call His servants to worship Him — as expressed in: *"Call to the way of your Lord with wisdom."*

This call is divided into two dimensions: outward practice, which is the natural bodily worship performed through obligatory duties and the Sunnahs the Prophet brought; and interpretation, which is knowledge — the great hidden, psychic worship

<!-- page 280 -->
peculiar to particular souls, taken from the perspective of the truths of belief, concerned with abstracting the monotheism of God so as to support the outward natural worship that is action. In the hermeneutical call, the sun has been appointed in its very being as the noblest of bodies, the source of light for all others, and the cause for the emergence of births in the natural world — a cause linked to all the causes of the world — and all of this is evidence pointing to the status of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family. Just as the sun is the noblest of bodies and the source of their light, so the speaking soul — the Prophet — is the most honored and most knowledgeable of souls. Just as the sun is the source of light for the other planets, so the Prophet, peace be upon him, is the source for human souls in the divine sciences that lead to monotheism. Just as the sun causes the birth of children in the natural world, so the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family, is the cause of spiritual births in the world of the soul. And just as the sun is a cause linked to the causes of the physical world, so the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, is a cause linked to the causes of the world of religion, which is the worship of Allah Almighty — one dimension in the body, the other in the soul.

As for the moon: it is ordered and receptive, drawing from the sun's light more than any other planet draws, illuminating the darkness of night, and standing as an intermediary between the sun and the birth of children through its acceptance and interaction. All of this points to the rank of the foundation — the Imam — for just as the moon ranks above other bodies in receiving light from the sun,

<!-- page 281 -->
so too the foundation ranks above others in receiving the light of creative existence from the Prophet, peace be upon him and his family. And just as the moon's reception of sunlight exceeds that of others, so the foundation's reception from the speaking Prophet, peace be upon him, of the blessings of monotheism that flow through him from Allah exceeds that of all other people. Just as the moon illuminates the darkness of night, so the foundation illuminates what the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, conveyed of the sealed symbols, the simplified law, and the multiplied parables. And just as the moon stands as an intermediary between the sun and others in the emergence of newborns, so too the foundation is an intermediary between the Prophet and the community, nurturing spiritual children in the world of the soul through mutual benefiting and instruction.

If the conditions of the sun and moon in the corporeal world correspond to the conditions of their spiritual counterparts in the world of religion, then we say: the moon passes through conditions, one of which is that when it meets the sun, it illuminates half of its mass on the side facing the sun — a state which astrologers hold releases a force that renders it virtually the cause of all events and entities until the next conjunction. This is like a minister meeting the king: the king commands him, and he in turn commands the kingdom to carry out whatever the king signs. Likewise, when the moon emerges from conjunction, this resembles the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him and his family, teaching the foundation, peace be upon him, whatever he wished to teach him so that the foundation could take his place in guidance, education, and the bestowal of benefit — a benefit the foundation could not convey before that meeting. This is why the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, pointed to Ali ibn Abi Talib and said: "I am the city of knowledge and he is its gate; whoever wishes to enter

<!-- page 282 -->
the city, let him come through the gate." Among the moon's conditions is its concealment after conjunction from the sight of the general public — it lies beneath the sun's rays and is hidden from all except those with the vision of knowledge. This is analogous to the foundation's position in what has been determined for him of his rank and the binding nature of his obedience: he is veiled from the guardians of ignorance and the common people, and none recognize him except those endowed with virtue and discernment. Another condition is the crescent's appearance when the sun sets from its rising point, so that both old and young can see it — this is like the appearance of the foundation's authority, as stipulated by the speaking Prophet, peace be upon him, and the binding of obedience to him when he emerged to the world, so that even the least could recognize the greatest. Another condition is that the moon has no light during the day when the sun is above the horizon — it has no presence until after the sun's setting — and this is like the foundation having no authority during the life of the Prophet, peace be upon them, until after the Prophet's departure from this world. Among the conditions is also that at the beginning of its appearance the crescent is weak, and only those with sharp sight can see it, and its sighting differs among people — this is like the weakness of the foundation's affair at the outset, and the divergence that occurred regarding his Imamate, with a group being told to obey him except those whose days were devoted sincerely. Among the conditions is the increase of its light as days pass,

and the gradual revelation of his Imamate among the people gaining strength over the years. Among the conditions is the completion of its light on the fourteenth night, when young and old see it or can hardly doubt its sighting — just as the light of his Imamate illuminates all nations when the seven repetitions are completed at the end of the cycle, so that no one doubts his leadership and precedence.

<!-- page 283 -->
Then the people of knowledge and faith affirm the day when he is hidden after conjunction with the coming month — just as those with true faith affirm what they knew of the Prophet's signs, peace and blessings be upon him, in recognizing the particular obedience owed to the Commander of the Faithful before it was explicitly stipulated for the general public. The ignorant and the common people, by contrast, doubt that day — unsure whether it should be fasted from the prior month or not — due to their limited knowledge, and so they break their fast until they see the crescent with their own eyes. Likewise, they regard the foundation's obedience as though it were no different from someone else's whose obedience is not obligatory — neither by sign nor by explicit text — and they violate obedience to him out of ignorance of its obligation, until the truth of his Imamate becomes plain to them.

The month of Ramadan is the most honored and greatest of the months of the year in terms of its importance, and it corresponds to the foundation's position, for Allah honored his rank through interpretation, which is knowledge, and marked it through signs. He is the one who gathered all the knowledge of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him. Therefore Allah Almighty said: *"The month of Ramadan in which the Quran was revealed as guidance to the people and as clear proofs of guidance and the criterion"* — as a sign pointing to the foundation. For we know that the Quran was revealed over twenty-three years, not all at once in the month of Ramadan; rather, it came down day by day, month by month, and year by year. The verse is therefore understood on its apparent face as pointing to signs, the criterion, and the distinction — and for this reason we need interpretation in order to reconcile the statement of Allah Almighty, the statement of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him and his family, and the obligation of fasting and its being binding upon the sighting of the crescent.

<!-- page 284 -->
From the perspective of the soul, this applies first to the people of knowledge when they sight it, and from the perspective of the senses to the people of ignorance — just as obedience to the foundation, peace be upon him, is obligatory upon the Prophet's departure, may Allah bless him and grant him this station, and his designation of the foundation through signs apprehended by knowledge and discernment, and through explicit text and formal declaration for the general public at the end. It is binding upon those who are present from the outset by way of instruction and learning, while they remain ahead of the wider community.

Hence Allah Almighty said: *"Do not raise your voices above the voice of the Prophet"* — addressing the community against transgression until the judgment of Allah Almighty is brought forth, and narrowing rather than widening it, drawing on the precedent of the Prophet in his time, and upon the foundation and their place, peace be upon them, so that the Imam is distinguished from those led, and the chief from those subordinate. Then He said: *"So whoever among you witnesses the month, let him fast"* — and this is a signal from the spokesman to the nation to follow the foundation and heed what he says, meaning: whoever attends the foundation's council of outward and hidden knowledge, let him be silent, for it is the foundation who is given the honor and entrusted with the affairs of religion and nothing else. This is proof that the people, before obedience to him became binding, used to engage freely in matters of religion and knowledge as they pleased — and they were then forbidden from it. Just as before fasting was obligatory they used to eat as they liked and were then forbidden from doing so through the binding of fasting, so the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, commanded likewise in the chapter of marriage, saying: "There is no marriage without a guardian and two witnesses" — indicating that marriage had been

<!-- page 285 -->
conducted without a guardian or two witnesses, as people did as they liked in the early days of Islam, and were then forbidden from it.

Then He said: *"And whoever is sick or on a journey, then a number of other days."* This is an exception to the ruling of witnessing the month, meaning that silence has been made obligatory for all people who attend the interpretive council attributed to the foundation — except for one whose words serve as a means of benefit, as one who questions and one who teaches, for whom remaining silent after drawing near, when attending that council, is the spiritual prayer. It is obligatory to observe the proper etiquette in the practical prayer of the Sunnah and in the establishment of rulings.

We have mentioned in the treatise that includes the discussion of fasting that it too is a means of explanation and clarification to the extent that benefits were made available to us from the side of Allah's guardian, peace be upon him. In the month of Ramadan of the year four hundred, by agreement, the people of truth entered their fast two days before the common sighting of the crescent moon, knowing with certainty that there was no discrepancy between the fasting of the people of truth and the fasting of the people of disagreement of more than one day. The matter was obscure to the ordinary mind except to the wise — and that is because the conjunction occurring in the month of Ramadan fell on the night of Monday, making it the first Monday, and the crescent was not seen on Tuesday night by looking for the light of its arc. At sunset it was at six degrees and some minutes above the horizon, and at such a distance — half of its mass — the sight cannot catch it because it falls beneath the blocking ray of the sun. So fasting was not broken that day, out of adherence to proper conduct. The moon was at its end on such degrees from the sun, and had one broken the fast that day,

<!-- page 286 -->
one would have fasted the month of Ramadan only twenty-nine days, because the conjunction of the sun and moon for Shawwal fell on the night of Wednesday at two hours and some minutes — making Wednesday the day of the forbidden Eid. A breach of one of the obligatory duties would then have occurred, since the month of Ramadan would have lasted thirty days without any diminishment of the words of the followers, peace of Allah be upon them: that there is no deficiency in Sha'ban or in Ramadan. The proof of the validity of our entering the fast on Monday is that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said: "Fast on the day of your fasting" — and the slaughter in the Haram fell on Monday the tenth of Dhu al-Hijjah in the year four hundred, based on the sighting through knowledge — which was the ninth of it based on the sighting by the eye. And the matter continues every year, as he said, peace be upon him.

A dispute arises if someone says: what I have mentioned of the hadith of the sun and the moon, and the degrees, the distances, and the hours — making all of this your guide — is something the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, forbade, namely taking the stars as one's guide and acting on them.

We say: the inference in establishing the time of fasting and determining it has come through the path of the sun and the moon, which are the cause of months and years — and this is only through their motion, their course, their conjunction, and their separation. Ending from it and drawing inference in establishing the qibla, knowing the times of prayer, and recognizing them is like fasting in being one of the pillars of Islam — and this is done through the lunar mansions and the height of the sun by the calculations

*"We have decreed for you"* — and is that anything other than the division of this measure? And it was not


<!-- page 287 -->
The Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) prohibited that pursuit only because of the risk that a student might chase astronomical knowledge without first grounding himself in the divine sciences — a danger that afflicted the followers of Ptolemy and those who attributed divinity to the celestial bodies. The legitimate religious sciences, by contrast, provide exactly what is needed: an understanding of how the heavens and the planets were arranged to deepen the soul's recognition of Allah's oneness, strengthening faith and sharpening the heart's perception of His unity. This is why Allah the Almighty said: "The creation of the heavens and the earth is greater than the creation of humanity, yet most people do not know." He also said: "We will show them Our signs on the horizons and within themselves until it becomes clear to them that it is the Truth." The horizons are nothing other than the heavens and all that they contain. Given all this, Allah the Almighty swore by the stars and identified them as a sign of His oneness in His Book, saying: "Blessed is He who placed constellations in the heavens and set therein a lamp." He swore by the positions of the stars, saying: "And I swear by the positions of the stars — and that, if you only knew, is a tremendous oath." He said: "I swear by the planets that recede, that run their course and set." He said: "By the sky adorned with the zodiac." He said: "By the sun and its radiance." He mentioned the sun and the moon together when He recited His signs, saying: "It is He who made the sun a light and the moon a light and determined its phases, so that you might know the number of years and the reckoning." Delaying one's knowledge of the planets' positions — their orbits, their peaks, and their nadirs — is to delay engagement with the very testimony to what Allah the Almighty has established of His religion, and to the

<!-- page 288 -->
truth of the Prophet's words (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) concerning what he conveyed from Allah. Such knowledge also encompasses the ranks of the degrees of awareness of what occurs as the planets move through their constellations — south and north — and the aspects they form with one another, affecting the world through the appearance of births and other events that point to the ranks of Allah's guardians. Are darkness and light equal? Are the blind and the seeing equal? Are those who know and those who do not know equal? Only those of understanding take heed.

From all the evidence we have set forth, it is established that the sighting is of two kinds, that acting upon the superior of the two in fulfilling one's obligations takes precedence, and that the Prophet's intention (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) in his use of the letter *lām* — in his saying that it is the sign to see it — was to direct the people's fasting to the sighting of the crescent with their eyes and nothing else. Know, then, and may Allah grant you success in obeying Him and obeying His guardians, the meanings of what we have set forth. Seek Allah's help in all your affairs, draw near to Him with sincere intention, and exert yourself in worshipping Him and obeying His guardians — for He is the best of helpers in attaining what one seeks to know of the realities. I thank Allah the Almighty for the blessings He has bestowed through His trustees.

The treatise concludes with praise of Allah the Almighty and prayers upon the Prophet Muhammad al-Mustafa, upon the guardian ʿAlī al-Murtada, upon the pure Imams — the moons amid the darkness — and upon the Imam of the age, our master, our guide, and the guardian of our blessings, the ruling Imam by the command of Allah, the Commander of the Faithful — may Allah's prayers be upon him and upon his pure forefathers and sons,

<!-- page 289 -->
the noble ones who are awaited until the Day of Judgment — the finest of greetings and salutations. We ask Allah to make us steadfast in obeying Him and His guardians, and to illuminate our minds and hearts through His generosity and grace. He is the best of those who are relied upon and the most generous of those in whom hope is placed. Allah is our sufficiency and the best of guardians, and the best of supporters. May Allah bless our master Muhammad and his family.

<!-- page 290 -->
